   25: # SQL comparison operators that are safe to interpolate into WHERE clauses.
   26: # Scalar-binding operators: the value is always passed as a bind param (?), so
   27: # no quoting or escaping is required on the value side.  LIKE and NOT LIKE are
   28: # safe for the same reason â€” the pattern is bound, not interpolated.
   29: Readonly::Hash my %SAFE_SQL_OPS => map { $_ => 1 } ('>', '<', '>=', '<=', '!=', '=', 'LIKE', 'NOT LIKE');
   30: 
   31: # List-membership operators whose criterion value is an arrayref.
   32: # Each element is bound as a separate ?, so injection is impossible.
   33: # IN  with an empty list â†’ WHERE 1=0 (no rows match, correct SQL semantics).
   34: # NOT IN with an empty list â†’ no WHERE term (all rows match, correct semantics).
   35: Readonly::Hash my %SAFE_LIST_OPS => map { $_ => 1 } ('IN', 'NOT IN');
   36: 
   37: # Nullability operators: no bind parameter.  The hashref value is ignored â€”
   38: # any value (undef, 1, ...) signals intent; only the key selects the operator.
   39: # A bare undef criterion value (col => undef) also generates IS NULL.
   40: Readonly::Hash my %SAFE_NOARG_OPS => map { $_ => 1 } ('IS NULL', 'IS NOT NULL');
   41: 
   42: # Key pushdown (array backend): the most primary keys that are passed to a
   43: # secondary database as one { -in => [...] } criterion.  Each key is a bind
   44: # parameter, and SQLite builds before 3.32 allow at most 999 in a statement.
   45: # Past this, reading the whole secondary is no worse than several queries.
   46: Readonly::Scalar my $MAX_PUSHDOWN_KEYS => 999;
   47: 
   48: # Database::Abstraction's spelling of SQL IN.  It croaks on 'IN'.
   49: Readonly::Scalar my $DA_IN_OP => '-in';
   50:

  116: 
  117: =head1 NAME
  118: 
  119: Database::Join - Read-only combined view across two or more Database::Abstraction objects
  120: 
  121: =head1 VERSION
  122: 
  123: Version 0.011.2
  124: 
  125: =head1 SYNOPSIS
  126: 
  127: B<Basic two-database join>
  128: 
  129:     use Database::Join;
  130: 
  131:     # Step 1: create each component database the normal way
  132:     # See Database::Abstraction
  133:     my $customers = Database::Customers->new(directory => '/data');
  134:     my $loyalty   = Database::Loyalty->new(directory  => '/data');
  135: 
  136:     # Step 2: combine them on the shared key column 'entry'
  137:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  138:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  139:         join_column => 'entry',
  140:     );
  141: 
  142:     # Step 3: query exactly as you would a single Database::Abstraction object
  143:     my $all_rows  = $join->selectall_arrayref();
  144:     my $vip_rows  = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold');
  145:     my $one_row   = $join->fetchrow_hashref(entry => 'C001');
  146:     my $total     = $join->count();
  147:     my $col_names = $join->columns();
  148: 
  149: B<Hiding internal columns>
  150: 
  151:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  152:         databases      => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  153:         join_column    => 'entry',
  154:         remove_columns => [ 'internal_id', 'audit_ts' ],
  155:     );
  156:     # 'internal_id' and 'audit_ts' never appear in results or columns()
  157: 
  158: B<join_map: when the key column has different names in each database>
  159: 
  160:     # $cities  (index 0) has a column called 'statecode' -- matches join_column
  161:     # $stnames (index 1) has a column called 'entry'     -- different name
  162: 
  163:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  164:         databases   => [ $cities,  $stnames ],
  165:         #                index 0   index 1
  166:         join_column => 'statecode',
  167:         join_map    => { 1 => 'entry' },  # index 1 calls its join key 'entry'
  168:     );
  169: 
  170:     # All returned rows use 'statecode'; 'entry' is never exposed
  171:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
  172: 
  173: B<filters: permanently restrict a database's visible rows>
  174: 
  175:     # Only show orders placed more than 60 days ago, without repeating
  176:     # the criterion on every query call.
  177:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  178:         databases   => [ $customers, $orders ],
  179:         join_column => 'entry',
  180:         filters     => { 1 => { age_days => { '>' => 60 } } },
  181:     );
  182: 
  183:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();                 # all old orders
  184:     my $vip  = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold');   # old + gold tier
  185: 
  186: B<Inner and outer join types>
  187: 
  188:     my $inner = Database::Join->new(
  189:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  190:         join_column => 'entry',
  191:         join_type   => 'inner',   # only keys present in BOTH databases
  192:     );
  193: 
  194:     my $outer = Database::Join->new(
  195:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  196:         join_column => 'entry',
  197:         join_type   => 'outer',   # all keys from EITHER database
  198:     );
  199: 
  200: B<Building the view incrementally with add_database>
  201: 
  202:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  203:         databases   => [ $customers ],
  204:         join_column => 'entry',
  205:     );
  206: 
  207:     $join->add_database($loyalty)
  208:          ->add_database($scores, remove_columns => ['raw_score']);
  209: 
  210: B<AUTOLOAD column shortcut>
  211: 
  212:     # Returns the 'name' value for entry 'C001' (scalar context)
  213:     my $name = $join->name(entry => 'C001');
  214: 
  215:     # Returns all 'tier' values (list context)
  216:     my @tiers = $join->tier();
  217: 
  218: B<aggregate(): GROUP BY across the merged view>
  219: 
  220:     # Count and total score per tier, highest total first
  221:     my $rows = $join->aggregate(
  222:         by       => ['tier'],
  223:         measures => [
  224:             { col => 'score', fn => 'sum',   as => 'total' },
  225:             {                 fn => 'count', as => 'n'     },
  226:         ],
  227:         sort_by  => ['total', 'DESC'],
  228:         having   => { total => { '>' => 100 } },
  229:     );
  230:     # => [ { tier => 'gold', total => 183, n => 2 }, ... ]
  231: 
  232:     # Grand total across the whole view (no 'by' key)
  233:     my $summary = $join->aggregate(
  234:         measures => [{ fn => 'count', as => 'n' }],
  235:     );
  236:     # => [ { n => 42 } ]
  237: 
  238: B<SQLite join backend for large datasets>
  239: 
  240:     # 'auto' (default): switches to SQLite automatically above the threshold
  241:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  242:         databases      => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  243:         join_column    => 'entry',
  244:         backend        => 'auto',          # default
  245:         max_array_rows => 50_000,          # use SQLite when combined rows > 50,000
  246:     );
  247: 
  248:     # Always use SQLite -- useful when you know the data is large
  249:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  250:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  251:         join_column => 'entry',
  252:         backend     => 'sqlite',
  253:         tmpdir      => '/fast/nvme/tmp',   # optional: faster temp disk
  254:     );
  255: 
  256:     # Always use the original in-memory path
  257:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  258:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  259:         join_column => 'entry',
  260:         backend     => 'array',
  261:     );
  262: 
  263: =head1 DESCRIPTION
  264: 
  265: C<Database::Join> merges two or more L<Database::Abstraction> objects into a
  266: single logical, read-only view.  Each component database is queried
  267: independently through its own C<Database::Abstraction> interface.  The results
  268: are combined using a shared key column (C<join_column>).
  269: In effect, this means that you can view data from more than one database using
  270: an intuitive, non-SQL interface.
  271: 
  272: Every storage format that C<Database::Abstraction> supports works as a
  273: component database: CSV, PSV, TSV, SQLite, JSON, XML, XLSX, BerkeleyDB, HTML
  274: URL, JSON URL, or any custom subclass.  Component databases may mix formats
  275: within the same join.
  276: 
  277: The module exposes the same read-only API as C<Database::Abstraction>:
  278: C<selectall_arrayref>, C<selectall_array>, C<fetchrow_hashref>, C<count>,
  279: C<columns>, C<schema>, C<updated>, C<set_logger>, and the AUTOLOAD column
  280: shortcut.  Callers do not need to know how many underlying databases are
  281: involved.
  282: 
  283: Think of it as a virtual database table that is assembled on demand from
  284: several real tables, one per component database.
  285: 
  286: B<Join backends>
  287: 
  288: By default (C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'>), C<Database::Join> first checks the
  289: combined source row count.  For small datasets (up to C<max_array_rows>,
  290: default 10,000 rows) it merges entirely in Perl memory.  For larger datasets it
  291: automatically spills source rows into a temporary SQLite database and executes
  292: a single SQL JOIN there, keeping peak RAM to roughly one times the source data
  293: size instead of three.  You can also force either path unconditionally with
  294: C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'> or C<backend =E<gt> 'array'>.
  295: 
  296: B<Key pushdown (array backend)>
  297: 
  298: Under a C<left> or C<inner> join, only secondary rows whose key is one of the
  299: primary's can appear in the result.  So once the primary has been read, each
  300: secondary that has no criterion on its join column is asked for just those
  301: keys, as C<< { -in => [ ... ] } >> on its local join column, instead of being
  302: read in full.  Joining a few hundred primary rows to a large secondary table
  303: then costs a few hundred rows, not the whole table.  Pushdown is skipped when:
  304: 
  305: =over 4
  306: 
  307: =item * the join is C<outer>, which needs every secondary key;
  308: 
  309: =item * the primary has no rows, or more than 999 keys (the bind-parameter
  310: limit of older SQLite builds; past that, reading the whole table is no worse);
  311: 
  312: =item * the query already has a criterion on the join column, which then
  313: stands as given;
  314: 
  315: =item * the secondary's C<selectall_arrayref> is not
  316: C<Database::Abstraction>'s own.  A subclass that overrides it, or another
  317: object implementing the interface, may not understand C<-in>.
  318: 
  319: =back
  320: 
  321: If the secondary croaks on the C<-in> query, it is read in full instead.  The
  322: result is the same either way.  Pushdown only narrows what is fetched; it never
  323: counts as the secondary's own criterion, so it can't turn a C<left> join into
  324: an C<inner> one.
  325: 
  326: =head2 Join semantics
  327: 
  328: The C<join_type> parameter controls what happens when a particular key value
  329: exists in some component databases but not all:
  330: 
  331: =over 4
  332: 
  333: =item C<left> (the default)
  334: 
  335: All rows from the I<primary> (first) database are returned.  Columns from
  336: subsequent databases are included where a matching row is found, and simply
  337: absent from the hashref where there is no match.  If you are familiar with
  338: SQL, this is a LEFT OUTER JOIN on the first table.
  339: 
  340: =item C<inner>
  341: 
  342: Only rows whose join-column value is present in I<every> component database
  343: are returned.  This is equivalent to a SQL INNER JOIN.
  344: 
  345: =item C<outer>
  346: 
  347: Every join-column value found in I<any> component database is returned.
  348: Columns from databases that do not have that key value are absent from the
  349: merged row.  This is a FULL OUTER JOIN.
  350: 
  351: =back
  352: 
  353: B<Important override rule:> whenever you pass a query criterion for a column
  354: that belongs to a secondary database, that database automatically acts as an
  355: inner-join partner for that query only -- regardless of C<join_type>.  This
  356: gives WHERE-clause semantics.  For example, if you have a LEFT join but query
  357: C<< tier => 'gold' >> on a secondary database, only rows whose secondary entry
  358: has tier = 'gold' are returned (rows with no secondary entry are excluded, just
  359: as a WHERE clause would exclude them).
  360: 
  361: =head2 Column ownership and routing
  362: 
  363: At construction time, C<Database::Join> calls C<columns()> on each component
  364: database and builds an internal index that maps every column name to the
  365: database that owns it.
  366: 
  367: When you pass criteria to a query method, each key-value pair is automatically
  368: routed to the right database.  You never need to say which database a column
  369: belongs to.
  370: 
  371: The C<join_column> is special: criteria on it are broadcast to I<all>
  372: databases so that each database fetches only the relevant rows before the
  373: in-memory merge.
  374: 
  375: When the same non-join column name exists in more than one database, the
  376: I<last> database in the C<databases> array wins by default: its value
  377: overwrites earlier ones in merged rows.  Use C<collision_prefix> to
  378: preserve both values under distinct names instead.
  379: 
  380: =head1 LIMITATIONS
  381: 
  382: =over 4
  383: 
  384: =item Memory usage (array backend)
  385: 
  386: When C<backend> is C<'array'> (or C<'auto'> and the dataset is small), all
  387: matching rows are fetched into Perl memory.  Key pushdown (see
  388: L</DESCRIPTION>) limits a secondary to the primary's keys under C<left> and
  389: C<inner> joins, but an C<outer> join, or a primary with more than 999 keys,
  390: still reads every secondary row.  Peak RAM is roughly three times
  391: the source data size.  For large datasets use C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'>, or
  392: leave C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'> and set C<max_array_rows> appropriately.
  393: 
  394: =item SQLite backend uses a persistent cache file
  395: 
  396: When the SQLite path is active, a single C<.db> file with a randomly generated
  397: name (chosen by C<File::Temp> to avoid collisions) is created in C<tmpdir> the
  398: first time a query runs on a given C<Database::Join> object.  Source data is
  399: spilled into that file once; subsequent queries against the same object reuse
  400: the file without re-fetching the source data.
  401: 
  402: The cache is automatically invalidated and rebuilt whenever any source
  403: database's C<updated()> timestamp changes (indicating new data), or when
  404: C<add_database()> is called.
  405: 
  406: The file is deleted when the C<Database::Join> object is destroyed (typically
  407: when it goes out of scope).  At any given moment no more than one such file
  408: exists per object.  The directory must be writable and have enough free space
  409: for the full source data (once, not per-query).
  410: 
  411: =item No chained builder or raw SQL
  412: 
  413: C<query()> and C<execute()> are not implemented.  Use C<selectall_arrayref>
  414: or C<fetchrow_hashref> instead.
  415: 
  416: =item Single-column equi-join only
  417: 
  418: Joining on more than one column simultaneously, or on expressions, is not
  419: supported.  When the join key has different names in different databases,
  420: use C<join_map> to declare each database's local column name.
  421: 
  422: =item Sort order
  423: 
  424: Results are sorted ascending by C<join_column> by default.  Pass
  425: C<< sort_by => 'colname' >> (or C<< sort_by => ['colname', 'DESC'] >>)
  426: to any query method to override this.  The array path uses string comparison
  427: (C<cmp>); for accurate numeric ordering on large datasets use the SQLite
  428: backend, which sorts natively by type.
  429: 
  430: =item count() on the array backend fetches all rows
  431: 
  432: On the array backend, C<count()> executes the full in-memory join and counts
  433: the resulting rows in Perl; no C<COUNT(*)> is pushed to the component
  434: databases.  On the SQLite backend, C<count()> executes a C<SELECT COUNT(*)>
  435: SQL query against the cached join tables, avoiding a full row transfer.
  436: 
  437: =back
  438: 
  439: =head1 COMMON PITFALLS
  440: 
  441: =over 4
  442: 
  443: =item The join_column must exist in every component database
  444: 
  445: If even one database is missing the join key column, C<new()> (or
  446: C<add_database()>) will C<croak> immediately.  Use C<join_map> when the
  447: column has a different local name in some databases.
  448: 
  449: =item Criteria on a removed column are silently dropped
  450: 
  451: If you call C<remove_column('tier')> and later query
  452: C<< selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold') >>, the criterion is ignored (with a
  453: C<carp> warning) and all rows are returned.  Always pass criteria before
  454: removing columns, or restructure your code to avoid this.
  455: 
  456: =item You cannot remove the join column
  457: 
  458: C<< $join->remove_column($join->join_column) >> will C<croak>.  The join key
  459: is required for the merge to work.
  460: 
  461: =item Left join does not guarantee all columns are populated
  462: 
  463: Under a LEFT join, rows from the primary database that have no matching row
  464: in a secondary database will be returned with I<no keys> from that secondary
  465: database.  Accessing C<< $row->{score} >> on such a row returns C<undef> --
  466: not zero, not an empty string.  Always test C<defined $row->{score}> rather
  467: than just C<$row->{score}> when the secondary match is optional.
  468: 
  469: =item Filters act as inner-join partners
  470: 
  471: Any database that has a C<filters> entry is promoted to an inner-join partner,
  472: regardless of C<join_type>.  A row whose join-key value does not appear in
  473: the filtered database's result is removed from the merged output entirely, not
  474: merely missing its secondary columns.  This is intentional but can be
  475: surprising if you expected LEFT join semantics.
  476: 
  477: =item Criteria-merging replaces scalar filters
  478: 
  479: When both a base filter and a query criterion target the same column, and both
  480: are operator hashrefs (e.g. C<< { '>' => 60 } >>), the operators are combined
  481: (AND semantics).  But if the query criterion is a plain scalar (e.g.
  482: C<< score => 75 >>), it I<replaces> the base filter for that column entirely --
  483: the base filter is ignored for that query.
  484: 
  485: =item AUTOLOAD sees the full merged join when filters or join_map are active
  486: 
  487: When either C<filters> or C<join_map> is in effect, the AUTOLOAD shortcut
  488: (C<< $join->columnname(...) >>) runs the full join query rather than
  489: delegating directly to the owning database.  This is necessary for correctness
  490: but means the result respects all active filters and join-key translations,
  491: which may differ from what the owning database would return on its own.
  492: 
  493: =item Duplicate column names: last database wins (unless collision_prefix is set)
  494: 
  495: When two component databases each have a column called C<notes>, the second
  496: database's value silently overwrites the first in every merged row.  Use
  497: C<collision_prefix => { 1 => 'right' }> to publish the second database's
  498: C<notes> as C<right.notes> so both values survive, or use C<remove_columns>
  499: (or C<remove_column>) to drop the unwanted duplicate entirely.
  500: 
  501: =item Mutating the filters hashref after construction has no effect
  502: 
  503: C<Database::Join> deep-copies the C<filters> hashref (and any C<filter>
  504: passed to C<add_database>) at the moment of construction.  The original hashref
  505: you passed in is never stored.  If you later modify it -- for example, to
  506: tighten or loosen a filter criterion -- the joined view is I<not> affected.
  507: Construct a new C<Database::Join> object, or use a component
  508: C<Database::Abstraction> that supports dynamic filter modification.
  509: 
  510: =item auto mode may always use the array path for some DAs
  511: 
  512: C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'> counts rows cheaply only when each component database
  513: either implements C<dbi_source()> (SQLite-backed) or directly defines a
  514: C<count()> method in its own package.  A DA that merely I<inherits> C<count()>
  515: from C<Database::Abstraction> is treated as uncountable, because the parent
  516: class C<count()> expects a key argument and behaves differently from a
  517: "return total row count" function.  In that case C<Database::Join>
  518: conservatively uses the array path for the whole query, even if the dataset is
  519: large.  To opt in to the SQLite path for such a DA, either add your own
  520: C<count()> override that returns the total row count, implement C<dbi_source()>,
  521: or use C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'> unconditionally.
  522: 
  523: =item dbi_source() ATTACH is unconditional - query-time criteria go into WHERE
  524: 
  525: When a component database implements C<dbi_source()>, C<Database::Join> always
  526: uses the zero-copy ATTACH path, I<even when the current query includes criteria
  527: for columns in that database>.  The criteria are translated into parameterised
  528: SQL C<WHERE> clauses applied against the ATTACHed table; no row-level copy is
  529: performed.  (Prior to 0.005.0 the presence of any query-time criteria would
  530: force a spill; that restriction has been removed.)
  531: 
  532: =item Broadcast join-column criterion does not force secondaries into inner-join
  533: 
  534: When a caller passes a join-column criterion (e.g. C<entry =E<gt> 'k1'>),
  535: C<Database::Join> broadcasts it to all component databases so each DA can
  536: filter its fetch to the requested key.  Prior to 0.006.0 this broadcast was
  537: incorrectly counted as "having criteria" for secondary databases, causing
  538: C<left> and C<outer> joins to silently behave as C<inner> joins when a
  539: join-column criterion was present.  The fix: only non-join-column criteria
  540: (e.g. column filters from the caller or base C<filters =E<gt> {...}>) promote
  541: a secondary to inner-join status.  The broadcast itself is now a transparent
  542: key-range selector that does not affect join semantics.
  543: 
  544: =item LIKE and NOT LIKE work on the SQLite path; other pattern operators do not yet
  545: 
  546: C<LIKE>, C<NOT LIKE>, C<IN>, and C<NOT IN> are fully supported on the SQLite
  547: backend.  C<LIKE>/C<NOT LIKE> take a scalar pattern; C<IN>/C<NOT IN> take an
  548: arrayref of values.  All are injection-safe because values are passed as bind
  549: parameters, never interpolated.
  550: 
  551:     # LIKE
  552:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(name => { LIKE => 'A%' });
  553: 
  554:     # IN
  555:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => { IN => ['gold', 'silver'] });
  556: 
  557:     # NOT IN
  558:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => { 'NOT IN' => ['bronze'] });
  559: 
  560: C<IN> with an empty arrayref matches no rows (SQL semantics: C<IN ()> is
  561: always false).  C<NOT IN> with an empty arrayref matches all rows (no
  562: constraint added).
  563: 
  564: C<IS NULL> and C<IS NOT NULL> are supported on the SQLite backend using an
  565: explicit operator hashref:
  566: 
  567:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(score => { 'IS NULL'     => undef });
  568:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(score => { 'IS NOT NULL' => 1    });
  569: 
  570: The hashref value is ignored; only the key selects the operator.  A bare
  571: C<undef> criterion value (C<< score => undef >>) also generates C<IS NULL>
  572: on the SQLite path.  Note: on the in-memory array path, the component DA
  573: may treat an C<undef> criterion value as C<< no filter >> rather than
  574: C<IS NULL>, so use the explicit hashref form for consistent behaviour
  575: across backends.
  576: 
  577: Any operator not in the supported set is skipped on the SQLite path with a
  578: C<carp> warning.  The array path forwards all operators to the component DA
  579: unchanged, which may or may not honour them.
  580: 
  581: =item Temp file directory must be writable and have free space
  582: 
  583: The SQLite path creates one temporary C<.db> file per C<Database::Join> object
  584: in C<tmpdir> (default: C<File::Spec-E<gt>tmpdir()>, usually C</tmp> on Unix).
  585: The filename is randomly generated by C<File::Temp> -- you cannot predict it,
  586: only the directory is under your control.  The file is created on the first
  587: query and kept alive until the object is destroyed; it is not re-created on
  588: every query call.  If the directory is not writable, or the filesystem is full,
  589: the call will C<croak> with C<error_sqlite_connect>.  Check permissions and
  590: free space if you see that error.
  591: 
  592: =item C<join =E<gt>> criteria are not supported
  593: 
  594: C<Database::Abstraction> accepts a C<join =E<gt> { table =E<gt> ..., on =E<gt> ... }>
  595: key in its criteria hashrefs to express an SQL JOIN within a single table.
  596: C<Database::Join> cannot route this to a component DA meaningfully: the merged
  597: view has no concept of a single underlying table.  Passing C<join =E<gt>> to any
  598: query method will C<croak> with C<error_join_criterion>.
  599: 
  600: B<Fix:> Model the joined table as a separate C<Database::Abstraction> object and
  601: add it to the C<databases =E<gt> []> list.  Use C<join_column> or C<join_map> to
  602: specify the shared key.
  603: 
  604: =item aggregate(): omitting C<by> gives a grand total, not an error
  605: 
  606: Calling C<aggregate()> without a C<by> key (or with C<< by => [] >>) does
  607: I<not> croak.  It returns a single-element arrayref containing one row with
  608: all the computed measure aliases.  This is a grand total over the entire view
  609: (possibly filtered by any extra criteria you pass).
  610: 
  611:     my $total = $join->aggregate(measures => [{ fn => 'count', as => 'n' }]);
  612:     # $total->[0]{n} is the total row count
  613: 
  614: If you expected multiple rows and got one, you probably forgot the C<by> key.
  615: 
  616: =item aggregate(): non-numeric values behave differently across backends
  617: 
  618: On the array backend (C<backend =E<gt> 'array'>), any non-numeric value in a
  619: column targeted by C<sum> or C<avg> is I<skipped> (not counted, not coerced),
  620: and a C<carp> warning C<warn_aggregate_non_numeric> is emitted.  On the SQLite
  621: backend, non-numeric values are silently coerced to C<0.0> via SQL C<CAST>.
  622: The two backends therefore produce different numeric totals when the data
  623: contains non-numeric values.  Clean your data first if you need consistent
  624: results across backends, or choose a backend explicitly.
  625: 
  626: =item aggregate(): C<BETWEEN> is not supported in C<having>
  627: 
  628: The C<having> hashref uses the same operator vocabulary as C<selectall_arrayref>,
  629: which does not include C<BETWEEN>.  Write a range as two explicit constraints:
  630: 
  631:     # Correct
  632:     having => { total => { '>=' => 10, '<=' => 50 } }
  633: 
  634:     # Wrong -- will croak with error_aggregate_having_operator
  635:     having => { total => { BETWEEN => [10, 50] } }
  636: 
  637: =item aggregate(): C<count> without C<col> counts all rows, not distinct non-NULL values
  638: 
  639: A measure with C<< fn => 'count' >> and no C<col> key emits C<COUNT(*)> on the
  640: SQLite backend (or counts every row in Perl).  This counts rows that have
  641: C<undef>/NULL in other columns too.  To count only rows where a specific
  642: column is non-NULL, or to count distinct values, use C<count_distinct>:
  643: 
  644:     # Count rows with a non-NULL 'score' (one way -- use a pre-filter)
  645:     measures => [{ col => 'score', fn => 'count_distinct', as => 'distinct_scores' }]
  646: 
  647: =item aggregate(): the result is never cached
  648: 
  649: Unlike C<selectall_arrayref> (which benefits from the SQLite file cache on
  650: repeated identical calls), C<aggregate()> always re-executes.  If your
  651: aggregation is expensive and the data changes infrequently, cache the result
  652: yourself using C<updated()> as a staleness signal.
  653: 
  654: =item distinct => 1 without columns returns full rows, not a column projection
  655: 
  656: C<< distinct => 1 >> alone deduplicates on I<all> columns of the merged row.
  657: If you want a distinct list of values for a single column (e.g. all unique
  658: tiers), you B<must> supply C<< columns => ['tier'] >> as well.  Without
  659: C<columns>, every column participates in the key used to detect duplicates,
  660: which means two rows that differ in any column (e.g. C<entry>) are never
  661: considered duplicates, and the result will be the same as without C<distinct>.
  662: 
  663: =item distinct => 1 with columns projects rows to those columns
  664: 
  665: When C<columns> is supplied alongside C<distinct>, each returned row contains
  666: I<only> the named columns -- all other columns are dropped.  The projection
  667: happens before deduplication, so the result set may be smaller than the full
  668: merged row count.  If you need the full row I<and> want to know unique values,
  669: run two separate queries or post-process in Perl.
  670: 
  671: =item mandatory_filters must never come from user-supplied input
  672: 
  673: The predicates in C<mandatory_filters> form an enforcement boundary.  They must
  674: come from a trusted server-side source (a session store, a policy table, or
  675: hard-coded application configuration) -- never from URL parameters, form fields,
  676: HTTP headers, or other user-controlled input.  A hostile user who can control
  677: a mandatory_filters predicate can widen it to C<{ col => 'status', op => 'ne',
  678: val => 'NONEXISTENT' }> and receive all rows regardless of status.
  679: 
  680: =item mandatory_filters uses string comparison on the array path
  681: 
  682: On the array backend, all C<mandatory_filters> comparisons (including C<lt>,
  683: C<lte>, C<gt>, C<gte>) are I<lexicographic string comparisons>, not numeric.
  684: C<< { col => 'score', op => 'gt', val => 9 } >> will match a score of C<'10'>
  685: only if C<'10' gt '9'> is true -- which it is not (C<'1' lt '9'>).  Use the
  686: SQLite backend (C<< backend => 'sqlite' >>) when numeric comparisons are needed,
  687: as SQLite uses the column's declared type for ordering.
  688: 
  689: =back
  690: 
  691: =head1 METHODS
  692: 
  693: =head2 new
  694: 
  695: =head3 SYNOPSIS
  696: 
  697:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  698:         databases         => [ $db1, $db2 ],
  699:         join_column       => 'entry',
  700:         join_type         => 'left',
  701:         join_map          => { 1 => 'local_col' },
  702:         filters           => { 1 => { score => { '>' => 60 } } },
  703:         base_criteria     => { active => 1 },
  704:         mandatory_filters => [{ col => 'region', op => 'eq', val => 'West' }],
  705:         collision_prefix  => { 1 => 'right' },
  706:         remove_columns    => [ 'email', 'internal_id' ],
  707:         backend           => 'auto',        # 'auto' | 'sqlite' | 'array'
  708:         max_array_rows    => 10_000,        # threshold for 'auto' mode
  709:         tmpdir            => '/tmp',        # directory for temp SQLite file
  710:         logger            => $log,
  711:         i18n              => $locale,
  712:     );
  713: 
  714: =head3 DESCRIPTION
  715: 
  716: Constructs and returns a new C<Database::Join> object.
  717: 
  718: Each element of C<databases> must be an already-instantiated subclass of
  719: C<Database::Abstraction>.  The constructor calls C<columns()> on every
  720: database to build an internal column-routing table and verifies that
  721: C<join_column> (or its local alias from C<join_map>) is present in each one.
  722: 
  723: Columns listed in C<remove_columns> are hidden immediately: they do not appear
  724: in C<columns()>, C<schema()>, or any returned row hashref.  This is equivalent
  725: to calling C<remove_column> once per name after construction.
  726: 
  727: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
  728: 
  729: =head4 Input
  730: 
  731:     databases      => { type => 'arrayref', required => 1 }
  732:                       # One or more Database::Abstraction subclass objects.
  733:                       #
  734:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   non-empty arrayref of blessed DA subclasses.
  735:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: scalar, hashref, or absent => croak.
  736:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA size:   minimum 1 element; no documented upper bound.
  737:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA elem:   each element must pass isa('Database::Abstraction').
  738: 
  739:     join_column    => { type => 'string',   optional => 1, default => 'entry' }
  740:                       # The column name shared by all databases (the join key).
  741:                       #
  742:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any non-empty string present in every component DA.
  743:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: column absent from any DA => croak join_col_missing.
  744:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        empty string '' is treated as a column name and
  745:                       #                       will croak if (as expected) it is absent from every DA.
  746:                       # DOMAIN -- NOTE:       matching is case-sensitive and exact.
  747: 
  748:     join_type      => { type => 'string',   optional => 1, default => 'left',
  749:                         enum => ['inner', 'left', 'outer'] }
  750:                       # Controls which keys appear in the result when not all
  751:                       # databases share the same key values.
  752:                       #
  753:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   exactly 'inner', 'left', or 'outer'.
  754:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: any other string including 'INNER', 'LEFT',
  755:                       #                       'OUTER' (enum check is case-sensitive), 'cross',
  756:                       #                       or '' => croak from validate_strict.
  757: 
  758:     join_map       => { type => 'hashref',  optional => 1 }
  759:                       # Zero-based database index => local column name.
  760:                       # See the join_map section for full details.
  761:                       #
  762:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   hashref values must be plain strings.
  763:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: reference value (hashref, arrayref, coderef, etc.)
  764:                       #                       => croak; the guard prevents heap-address leakage.
  765:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        out-of-range keys (beyond the databases array) are
  766:                       #                       silently ignored.
  767: 
  768:     filters        => { type => 'hashref',  optional => 1 }
  769:                       # Zero-based database index => criteria hashref.
  770:                       # Permanent row restrictions on individual databases.
  771:                       # See the filters section for full details.
  772: 
  773:     base_criteria  => { type => 'hashref',  optional => 1 }
  774:                       # Column name => value hashref.
  775:                       # Permanent criteria applied to every query, specified by
  776:                       # column name rather than database index.  Each key must
  777:                       # be a column that appears in the merged view (or the
  778:                       # join_column); each value is a plain scalar or an
  779:                       # operator hashref in the same format as
  780:                       # selectall_arrayref accepts.  Criteria are automatically
  781:                       # routed to the database that owns each column (same
  782:                       # routing used by selectall_arrayref).
  783:                       # Equivalent to filters but more convenient when you know
  784:                       # the column name but not which database index owns it.
  785:                       # When a column appears in both base_criteria and filters,
  786:                       # the filters entry takes precedence.
  787:                       # See the base_criteria section for full details.
  788:                       #
  789:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   hashref of column-name => scalar or
  790:                       #                       operator-hashref pairs.  Unknown column
  791:                       #                       names emit warn_unknown_column (carp)
  792:                       #                       and are silently dropped.
  793:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: non-hashref value => croak from
  794:                       #                       validate_strict.
  795:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        {} empty hashref is a safe no-op.
  796: 
  797:     collision_prefix => { type => 'hashref', optional => 1 }
  798:                       # Zero-based database index (>0) => prefix string.
  799:                       # When a secondary database has a column that collides
  800:                       # with a column already present in the merged view, the
  801:                       # secondary column is published as "$prefix.$col" instead
  802:                       # of silently overwriting the earlier value.
  803:                       # Index 0 entries are silently ignored.
  804:                       # Omitting this parameter preserves the original
  805:                       # last-database-wins behaviour.
  806:                       # See the collision_prefix section for full details.
  807:                       #
  808:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   absent or {} => last-database-wins (no change).
  809:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   { N => 'prefix' } where N > 0 => colliding
  810:                       #                       columns from DB[N] published as "$prefix.$col";
  811:                       #                       non-colliding columns from the same DB added plain.
  812:                       # DOMAIN -- EP note:    index-0 entries are silently ignored.
  813:                       # DOMAIN -- Invariant:  join_column is never prefixed regardless of
  814:                       #                       collision_prefix configuration.
  815: 
  816:     remove_columns => { type => 'arrayref', optional => 1 }
  817:                       # Column names to hide from the merged view.
  818:                       #
  819:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   arrayref of any strings; non-existent columns
  820:                       #                       are silently ignored (idempotent).
  821:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: join_column itself => croak remove_join_col.
  822:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        [] empty arrayref is a safe no-op.
  823: 
  824:     backend        => { type => 'string',   optional => 1, default => 'auto',
  825:                         enum => ['array', 'sqlite', 'auto'] }
  826:                       # Controls which join strategy is used.
  827:                       #   'auto'   -- (default) use 'array' when combined source row count
  828:                       #              <= max_array_rows, 'sqlite' otherwise.
  829:                       #   'sqlite' -- always spill to a temporary SQLite database.
  830:                       #   'array'  -- always use the in-memory merge path.
  831:                       #
  832:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   'array', 'sqlite', or 'auto' (case-sensitive).
  833:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: any other string => croak error_invalid_backend.
  834:                       # DOMAIN -- Default:    'auto'.
  835: 
  836:     max_array_rows => { type => 'integer',  optional => 1, default => 10_000 }
  837:                       # Row-count threshold for 'auto' mode.  When the combined
  838:                       # source row count exceeds this value, the SQLite path is used.
  839:                       # Ignored when backend is 'array' or 'sqlite'.
  840:                       #
  841:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any non-negative integer.
  842:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        0 means always use SQLite (all counts exceed 0).
  843:                       # DOMAIN -- Default:    10,000.
  844: 
  845:     tmpdir         => { type => 'string',   optional => 1 }
  846:                       # Directory for the per-call temporary SQLite database file.
  847:                       # The file is created securely by File::Temp and removed when
  848:                       # the query completes.  Ignored when backend is 'array'.
  849:                       #
  850:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any writable directory path string.
  851:                       # DOMAIN -- EP absent:  uses File::Spec->tmpdir() (system temp dir).
  852: 
  853:     parallel       => { type => 'integer',  optional => 1, default => 0 }
  854:                       # When set to 1 and the join has more than 2 databases (primary +
  855:                       # 2 or more secondaries), secondary DA fetches are issued in
  856:                       # parallel Perl threads.  Requires the 'threads' module; falls
  857:                       # back to sequential with a carp warning when unavailable.
  858:                       # Has no effect on the SQLite backend (which uses a single SQL
  859:                       # JOIN).  DBI-backed DAs are not thread-safe by default; only
  860:                       # enable this for in-memory or otherwise thread-safe DA backends.
  861:                       #
  862:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   0 (sequential, default) or 1 (parallel).
  863:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: any other integer is treated as truthy/falsy.
  864:                       # DOMAIN -- Default:    0.
  865: 
  866:     autoload_pk     => { type => 'string',   optional => 1 }
  867:                       # The column name used as the positional-argument key when
  868:                       # AUTOLOAD performs a full join query (i.e. when join_map or
  869:                       # filters is active).  Calling $join->col('value') with a bare
  870:                       # scalar maps that scalar to { autoload_pk => 'value' } before
  871:                       # routing through the merged view.
  872:                       #
  873:                       # Defaults to join_column when omitted.  Override when the
  874:                       # primary DA's natural lookup key differs from join_column -
  875:                       # for example, when join_column is 'statecode' but you want to
  876:                       # look up by city name ('entry').
  877:                       #
  878:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any string that is a visible column in
  879:                       #                       the merged view (checked at call time by
  880:                       #                       _partition_criteria; unknown names are
  881:                       #                       carp'd and dropped).
  882:                       # DOMAIN -- EP absent:  defaults to join_column.
  883: 
  884:     mandatory_filters => { type => 'arrayref', optional => 1 }
  885:                       # Arrayref of {col, op, val} predicate hashrefs.
  886:                       # These filters are applied to every query result regardless
  887:                       # of what criteria the caller supplies.  They cannot be
  888:                       # overridden, suppressed, or circumvented at query time.
  889:                       # Each predicate must supply:
  890:                       #   col -- a column name present in the merged view
  891:                       #   op  -- one of: eq, ne, lt, lte, gt, gte, like, contains
  892:                       #   val -- the comparison value (plain scalar)
  893:                       # On the array backend: applied as a Perl-side grep after the
  894:                       # in-memory merge, before sort/limit/offset.
  895:                       # On the SQLite backend: injected as parameterised WHERE clauses
  896:                       # into the per-call SQL query.
  897:                       #
  898:                       # SECURITY: Never set this from URL parameters or any
  899:                       # user-supplied value.  Predicates must come from the
  900:                       # server-side session or policy store only.
  901:                       #
  902:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   [] empty arrayref => no constraint (safe no-op).
  903:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   arrayref of {col, op, val} hashrefs where
  904:                       #                       col is in the merged view and op is supported.
  905:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: element is not a hashref, or is missing
  906:                       #                       col/op/val => croak error_mandatory_filter_ref.
  907:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: col not in merged view => croak error_mandatory_filter_col.
  908:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: op not in supported set => croak error_mandatory_filter_op.
  909:                       # DOMAIN -- ops:        eq (string eq), ne (string ne),
  910:                       #                       lt/lte/gt/gte (string comparison),
  911:                       #                       like (SQL LIKE pattern with % and _),
  912:                       #                       contains (substring match, equivalent to LIKE %val%).
  913: 
  914:     logger         => { type => 'object',   optional => 1 }
  915:                       # Logger object propagated to all component databases.
  916: 
  917:     i18n           => { type => 'object',   optional => 1 }
  918:                       # Localisation object with a translate($key, @args) method.
  919: 
  920: =head4 Output
  921: 
  922:     A blessed Database::Join object.
  923: 
  924: =head3 EXAMPLE
  925: 
  926:     # Customers database: entry | name | email
  927:     # Loyalty   database: entry | tier | points
  928: 
  929:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  930:         databases      => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  931:         join_column    => 'entry',
  932:         join_type      => 'inner',            # only customers who also have loyalty records
  933:         remove_columns => [ 'email' ],        # hide PII from query results
  934:         filters        => { 1 => { points => { '>' => 0 } } }, # ignore zero-point records
  935:     );
  936: 
  937:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
  938:     # Each row: { entry => ..., name => ..., tier => ..., points => ... }
  939:     # 'email' is absent. Zero-point loyalty records are excluded.
  940: 
  941: =head3 PSEUDOCODE
  942: 
  943:     validate all parameters with validate_strict
  944:     croak if databases is empty
  945:     croak if any element of databases is not a Database::Abstraction subclass
  946:     bless the object with all fields initialised
  947:     call _build_col_index to map every column to its owning database
  948:         and verify join_column presence in each database
  949:     if base_criteria given:
  950:         partition base_criteria by column ownership into per-db slices
  951:         for each database with a non-empty slice:
  952:             merge the slice into _filters[i]
  953:             (explicit filters win on plain-scalar conflicts)
  954:     if mandatory_filters given:
  955:         for each predicate in mandatory_filters:
  956:             croak error_mandatory_filter_ref if predicate is not a {col, op, val} hashref
  957:             croak error_mandatory_filter_col if col is not in the merged view
  958:             croak error_mandatory_filter_op  if op is not in the supported set
  959:         store shallow copies of all validated predicates in _mandatory_filters
  960:     for each column in remove_columns: call remove_column
  961:     return the new object
  962: 
  963: =head3 MESSAGES
  964: 
  965:     error_no_databases           -- databases arrayref was empty
  966:     error_invalid_db             -- an element of databases is not a D::A subclass
  967:     error_join_col_missing       -- join_column (or its join_map alias) not found in a database
  968:     error_invalid_backend        -- backend value is not 'array', 'sqlite', or 'auto'
  969:     error_sqlite_connect         -- temporary SQLite database could not be created (backend='sqlite'/'auto')
  970:     error_mandatory_filter_ref   -- a mandatory_filters element is not a hashref with col, op, and val keys
  971:     error_mandatory_filter_col   -- a mandatory_filters element names a column not in the merged view
  972:     error_mandatory_filter_op    -- a mandatory_filters element uses an unsupported op value
  973:     warn_schema_type_mismatch    -- (carp) a shared column has different types across databases;
  974:                                     use collision_prefix to preserve both values
  975: 
  976: =cut
  977:
