 1124: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 1125: # Public API (mirrors Database::Abstraction)
 1126: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 1127: 
 1128: =head2 join_map - joining on differently-named columns
 1129: 
 1130: By default every component database must have a column whose name matches
 1131: C<join_column>.  If a database uses a different local name for the join key,
 1132: declare the mapping with C<join_map>.
 1133: 
 1134: C<join_map> is a hashref.  Each B<key> is the B<zero-based position> of a
 1135: database in the C<databases> array (0 = first, 1 = second, and so on).  Each
 1136: B<value> is the name that B<that particular database> uses for the join key.
 1137: 
 1138: Databases not listed in C<join_map> are assumed to already have a column
 1139: named C<join_column> and need no entry.
 1140: 
 1141: Throughout the merged view the join key is I<always> referred to by the name
 1142: given in C<join_column>.  The local alias is never exposed in returned rows,
 1143: in C<columns()>, or in C<schema()>.
 1144: 
 1145: B<When do you need join_map?>
 1146: 
 1147: You need C<join_map> when you have two tables like:
 1148: 
 1149:     cities table  : entry (the city name) | statecode
 1150:     stnames table : entry (the state code) | state
 1151: 
 1152: Here you want to join cities.statecode to stnames.entry.  You choose
 1153: C<< join_column => 'statecode' >> as the canonical name, but stnames calls
 1154: that same concept C<entry>, so you declare:
 1155: 
 1156:     join_map => { 1 => 'entry' }  # stnames (index 1) calls it 'entry'
 1157: 
 1158: B<Example>
 1159: 
 1160:     #                        index 0     index 1
 1161:     my @databases = (       $cities,    $stnames  );
 1162:     #  join key column:    'statecode'  'entry'
 1163:     #  join_column:        'statecode' (chosen canonical name)
 1164:     #  stnames differs, so declare the alias:
 1165: 
 1166:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1167:         databases   => \@databases,
 1168:         join_column => 'statecode',
 1169:         join_map    => { 1 => 'entry' },
 1170:     );
 1171: 
 1172:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1173:     # Each $row has keys: entry (city), statecode, state
 1174:     # 'entry' from stnames is never exposed directly.
 1175: 
 1176:     my $row = $join->fetchrow_hashref(statecode => 'CA');
 1177: 
 1178: B<Using add_database instead>
 1179: 
 1180: If you build the join incrementally with C<add_database>, pass
 1181: C<join_column> directly to that call instead of using C<join_map>:
 1182: 
 1183:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1184:         databases   => [ $cities ],
 1185:         join_column => 'statecode',
 1186:     );
 1187:     $join->add_database($stnames, join_column => 'entry');
 1188: 
 1189: This is exactly equivalent to the C<join_map> form above.
 1190: 
 1191: =head2 filters - permanent per-database row filters
 1192: 
 1193: C<filters> lets you restrict a component database to a subset of its rows
 1194: permanently, without repeating the criterion on every query call.
 1195: 
 1196: Think of it as telling the join: "whenever you query this database, always
 1197: add these extra conditions".  Callers never need to specify the restriction
 1198: themselves and can never accidentally omit it.
 1199: 
 1200: C<filters> is a hashref.  Each B<key> is the B<zero-based position> of a
 1201: database in the C<databases> array (same numbering as C<join_map>).  Each
 1202: B<value> is a criteria hashref in the same format as C<selectall_arrayref>
 1203: accepts.
 1204: 
 1205: B<Key-set semantics>
 1206: 
 1207: A filtered database always acts as an inner-join partner, regardless of the
 1208: C<join_type> setting.  Any join-key value that does not pass the filter is
 1209: excluded from the merged output entirely -- not just missing its secondary
 1210: columns.  This ensures the filter genuinely restricts the view rather than
 1211: simply hiding a few fields.
 1212: 
 1213: B<Criteria merging>
 1214: 
 1215: When a query call also passes a criterion for a column that already has a base
 1216: filter, the two constraints are combined:
 1217: 
 1218: =over 4
 1219: 
 1220: =item *
 1221: 
 1222: When both the base filter value and the query criterion are operator hashrefs
 1223: (e.g. C<< { '>' => 60 } >> and C<< { '<' => 365 } >>), their operators are
 1224: merged: I<both> constraints apply simultaneously (AND semantics).
 1225: 
 1226: =item *
 1227: 
 1228: When either value is a plain scalar, or the operators conflict, the
 1229: query-time criterion wins and the base filter for that column is ignored for
 1230: that one call.
 1231: 
 1232: =back
 1233: 
 1234: B<Example -- only show orders placed more than 60 days ago>
 1235: 
 1236:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1237:         databases   => [ $customers, $orders ],
 1238:         join_column => 'entry',
 1239:         filters     => { 1 => { age_days => { '>' => 60 } } },
 1240:     );
 1241: 
 1242:     # Every query automatically sees only old orders
 1243:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1244: 
 1245:     # Additional criteria layer on top -- gold tier AND old order
 1246:     my $vip  = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold');
 1247: 
 1248:     # Range intersection: age_days > 60 AND age_days < 365
 1249:     my $mid  = $join->selectall_arrayref(age_days => { '<' => 365 });
 1250: 
 1251: When using C<add_database>, pass C<filter> (singular) to set the base
 1252: criteria for the new database:
 1253: 
 1254:     $join->add_database($orders, filter => { age_days => { '>' => 60 } });
 1255: 
 1256: =head2 base_criteria - permanent view-level row filters by column name
 1257: 
 1258: C<base_criteria> is a convenience alternative to C<filters> for callers who
 1259: know the column names they want to restrict but prefer not to track database
 1260: indices.
 1261: 
 1262:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1263:         databases     => [ $customers, $orders ],
 1264:         join_column   => 'entry',
 1265:         base_criteria => { active => 1, deleted_at => undef },
 1266:     );
 1267: 
 1268:     # Every query automatically sees only active, non-deleted rows.
 1269:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1270: 
 1271: At construction time C<base_criteria> is partitioned by column ownership
 1272: using the same routing logic as C<selectall_arrayref>.  Each criterion is
 1273: sent to the database that owns that column and stored as a permanent base
 1274: filter (equivalent to the corresponding C<filters> entry).
 1275: 
 1276: B<Unknown columns> emit a C<warn_unknown_column> carp and are silently
 1277: dropped, just as they would be in a query call.
 1278: 
 1279: B<Key-set semantics> are identical to C<filters>: any database that receives
 1280: a C<base_criteria> slice acts as an inner-join partner regardless of
 1281: C<join_type>.
 1282: 
 1283: B<Precedence>: when a column appears in both C<base_criteria> and C<filters>,
 1284: the C<filters> entry wins on plain-scalar conflicts; operator-hashref values
 1285: are combined with AND semantics.
 1286: 
 1287: B<Use cases>
 1288: 
 1289: =over 4
 1290: 
 1291: =item *
 1292: 
 1293: Row-level security: C<< base_criteria => { tenant_id => $tid } >>
 1294: 
 1295: =item *
 1296: 
 1297: Soft-delete filtering: C<< base_criteria => { deleted_at => undef } >>
 1298: 
 1299: =item *
 1300: 
 1301: Status gates: C<< base_criteria => { active => 1 } >>
 1302: 
 1303: =back
 1304: 
 1305: =head2 mandatory_filters - row-level security predicates that cannot be bypassed
 1306: 
 1307: C<mandatory_filters> is an arrayref of C<{col, op, val}> predicate hashrefs.
 1308: Every predicate is applied to the merged result of every query, regardless of
 1309: what criteria the caller passes.  Unlike C<filters> and C<base_criteria> (which
 1310: are per-database constraints applied at fetch time), C<mandatory_filters>
 1311: operates on the fully-merged row set, ensuring the constraint applies uniformly
 1312: even when the same logical value is spread across multiple databases.
 1313: 
 1314:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1315:         databases         => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
 1316:         join_column       => 'entry',
 1317:         mandatory_filters => [
 1318:             { col => 'region',  op => 'eq',  val => 'West' },
 1319:             { col => 'tier',    op => 'ne',  val => 'banned' },
 1320:             { col => 'balance', op => 'gte', val => 0        },
 1321:         ],
 1322:     );
 1323: 
 1324: Each predicate hashref must supply exactly these three keys:
 1325: 
 1326: =over 4
 1327: 
 1328: =item C<col>
 1329: 
 1330: The column name in the merged view (as returned by C<columns()>).  Must be
 1331: present at construction time; columns added later via C<add_database()> are not
 1332: retroactively covered by predicates already in C<_mandatory_filters>.
 1333: 
 1334: =item C<op>
 1335: 
 1336: The comparison operator.  Supported values:
 1337: 
 1338:     eq       -- string equality      ($row->{col} eq $val)
 1339:     ne       -- string inequality    ($row->{col} ne $val)
 1340:     lt       -- string less-than     ($row->{col} lt $val)
 1341:     lte      -- string <=            ($row->{col} le $val)
 1342:     gt       -- string greater-than  ($row->{col} gt $val)
 1343:     gte      -- string >=            ($row->{col} ge $val)
 1344:     like     -- SQL LIKE pattern     (% = any sequence, _ = any one character)
 1345:     contains -- substring match      (row value must contain val as a substring)
 1346: 
 1347: Comparisons are lexicographic (string-based) on the array path; on the SQLite
 1348: path they follow SQLite's default collation for the column type.  Use the
 1349: SQLite backend (C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'>) when numeric ordering matters.
 1350: 
 1351: =item C<val>
 1352: 
 1353: The right-hand side of the comparison.  Must be a plain scalar.
 1354: 
 1355: =back
 1356: 
 1357: B<Supported use cases>
 1358: 
 1359: =over 4
 1360: 
 1361: =item *
 1362: 
 1363: Row-level security: restrict the view to rows the current session is authorised
 1364: to see: C<< { col => 'tenant_id', op => 'eq', val => $session_tenant } >>
 1365: 
 1366: =item *
 1367: 
 1368: Data classification gates: hide rows marked with a sensitivity label:
 1369: C<< { col => 'classification', op => 'ne', val => 'restricted' } >>
 1370: 
 1371: =item *
 1372: 
 1373: Substring/pattern guards:
 1374: C<< { col => 'email', op => 'contains', val => '@example.com' } >>
 1375: or C<< { col => 'code', op => 'like', val => 'PROD_%' } >>
 1376: 
 1377: =back
 1378: 
 1379: B<SECURITY: C<mandatory_filters> must never be set from user-supplied values>
 1380: 
 1381: The predicates in C<mandatory_filters> are an enforcement boundary.  They must
 1382: come from a trusted server-side source (a session store, a policy table, or
 1383: hard-coded application configuration) -- never from URL parameters, form fields,
 1384: HTTP headers, or any other user-controlled input.  Setting this parameter from
 1385: user-supplied data would allow an attacker to widen or replace the constraints
 1386: and access rows they are not permitted to see.
 1387: 
 1388: B<Array vs. SQLite backend>
 1389: 
 1390: On the B<array backend>, C<mandatory_filters> is applied as a Perl-side C<grep>
 1391: after the in-memory merge, immediately before C<sort_by>/C<limit>/C<offset>.
 1392: A row that does not satisfy every predicate is silently dropped.
 1393: 
 1394: On the B<SQLite backend>, each predicate is translated into a parameterised
 1395: C<WHERE> clause and injected into the per-call SQL query.  Values are always
 1396: passed via DBI bind parameters, never by string interpolation.
 1397: 
 1398: Both backends produce identical filtered results.
 1399: 
 1400: =head2 distinct - deduplicate merged rows
 1401: 
 1402: Pass C<< distinct => 1 >> to C<selectall_arrayref> or C<selectall_array> to
 1403: remove duplicate rows from the merged result.
 1404: 
 1405:     # All unique tier values
 1406:     my $tiers = $join->selectall_arrayref(
 1407:         distinct => 1,
 1408:         columns  => ['tier'],
 1409:     );
 1410: 
 1411:     # All unique region + tier combinations
 1412:     my $combos = $join->selectall_arrayref(
 1413:         distinct => 1,
 1414:         columns  => ['region', 'tier'],
 1415:     );
 1416: 
 1417:     # Remove fully-identical merged rows (all columns participate)
 1418:     my $deduped = $join->selectall_arrayref(distinct => 1);
 1419: 
 1420: The optional C<columns> arrayref projects each row to the named columns I<before>
 1421: deduplication.  Omitting C<columns> (or passing an empty arrayref) deduplicates
 1422: on the full merged row: two rows must be identical in every column to be
 1423: considered duplicates.
 1424: 
 1425: B<Array backend>: deduplication is done in Perl using a seen-hash keyed on the
 1426: selected column values.  First occurrence wins.
 1427: 
 1428: B<SQLite backend>: C<SELECT DISTINCT col1, col2, ...> is used; the SQL engine
 1429: handles deduplication.
 1430: 
 1431: Both backends produce identical results.
 1432: 
 1433: B<Interaction with sort_by / limit / offset>: C<distinct> is applied first
 1434: (before pagination and ordering), so C<limit> and C<offset> operate on the
 1435: already-deduplicated set.
 1436: 
 1437: =head2 collision_prefix - preserve colliding columns from secondary databases
 1438: 
 1439: By default, when a column name appears in more than one database the I<last>
 1440: database wins: its value silently overwrites earlier ones in merged rows.
 1441: This loses data and makes the origin invisible.
 1442: 
 1443: C<collision_prefix> changes this for secondary databases you designate.
 1444: When a secondary database at index N has a column that already exists in the
 1445: merged view, and C<collision_prefix-E<gt>{N}> is set, the colliding column is
 1446: published as C<"$prefix.$col"> instead of overwriting.  Both values are then
 1447: visible: the original column keeps its name (from the earlier database), and
 1448: the collision gets the prefixed name.
 1449: 
 1450: C<collision_prefix> is a hashref.  Each B<key> is the B<zero-based index> of
 1451: a secondary database in the C<databases> array (same numbering as C<join_map>).
 1452: Each B<value> is the prefix string to prepend.  An index-0 entry is
 1453: meaningless and silently ignored.  Omitting C<collision_prefix> entirely
 1454: preserves the previous last-wins behaviour and changes nothing.
 1455: 
 1456: Non-colliding columns from a secondary database are always added as-is with
 1457: no prefix, whether or not C<collision_prefix> is configured.
 1458: 
 1459: B<Example -- sales table and products table, both with a "product" column>
 1460: 
 1461:     # $sales    columns: id, product, amount, date
 1462:     # $products columns: sku, product, price, category
 1463:     # join on 'product' (left key) matched against 'sku' (right key via join_map)
 1464: 
 1465:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1466:         databases        => [$sales, $products],
 1467:         join_column      => 'product',
 1468:         join_map         => { 1 => 'sku' },
 1469:         collision_prefix => { 1 => 'products' },
 1470:     );
 1471: 
 1472:     $join->columns;
 1473:     # => ['amount', 'category', 'date', 'id', 'price', 'product', 'products.product']
 1474:     #                                                               ^-- prefixed collision
 1475: 
 1476:     my $row = $join->fetchrow_hashref(product => 'widget');
 1477:     # $row->{product}            -- value from $sales
 1478:     # $row->{'products.product'} -- value from $products (different row, same column name)
 1479:     # $row->{price}              -- from $products, no collision, kept as-is
 1480: 
 1481: B<Querying on a prefixed column>
 1482: 
 1483: Use the full published name as the criterion key:
 1484: 
 1485:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref('products.product' => 'widget');
 1486:     # Internally routes as: product => 'widget' to $products
 1487: 
 1488: B<Interaction with remove_column>
 1489: 
 1490: C<remove_column> operates on published names.  To suppress a prefixed
 1491: collision column entirely, pass the prefixed name:
 1492: 
 1493:     $join->remove_column('products.product');
 1494: 
 1495: =head2 backend - SQLite join backend for large datasets
 1496: 
 1497: C<Database::Join> can merge component databases in two different ways,
 1498: controlled by the C<backend> constructor parameter.
 1499: 
 1500: =over 4
 1501: 
 1502: =item C<backend =E<gt> 'array'> -- in-memory merge (original behaviour)
 1503: 
 1504: All matching rows are fetched from every component database into Perl hashes
 1505: and merged there.  Simple and fast for small and medium datasets.  Peak RAM
 1506: is roughly three times the combined source data size (one copy per database
 1507: plus one merged copy).
 1508: 
 1509: =item C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'> -- SQL JOIN via a cached temporary file
 1510: 
 1511: C<Database::Join> creates a temporary SQLite database file, spills source
 1512: rows into it (one table per component database), then executes a single SQL
 1513: C<JOIN> statement per query call.  Peak RAM drops to roughly one times the
 1514: source data size.
 1515: 
 1516: The temporary file is created once and reused across multiple query calls on
 1517: the same object (the cache).  Only query-time criteria vary per call; they
 1518: are applied as SQL C<WHERE> clauses against the cached data.  The cache is
 1519: automatically rebuilt when any source database's C<updated()> timestamp
 1520: changes.  The file is deleted when the object is destroyed (goes out of
 1521: scope).  See I<Temporary file: name, location, and lifetime> below for
 1522: details.
 1523: 
 1524: Requires C<DBD::SQLite E<gt>= 1.70> (C<FULL OUTER JOIN> support was added in
 1525: SQLite 3.39.0; DBD::SQLite 1.70 ships SQLite 3.39.2).
 1526: 
 1527: =item C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'> (default)
 1528: 
 1529: C<Database::Join> counts the total rows from all component databases cheaply
 1530: -- without fetching them -- and then decides:
 1531: 
 1532: =over 4
 1533: 
 1534: =item *
 1535: 
 1536: If the combined count is less than or equal to C<max_array_rows> (default
 1537: 10,000), use the array path.
 1538: 
 1539: =item *
 1540: 
 1541: If the combined count exceeds C<max_array_rows>, use the SQLite path.
 1542: 
 1543: =back
 1544: 
 1545: For counting to work without fetching, each component database must either
 1546: implement the C<dbi_source()> interface (for SQLite-backed sources, where a
 1547: C<COUNT(*)> SQL query is issued directly), or directly define a C<count()>
 1548: method in its own package -- not just inherit one from a parent class.  If
 1549: neither is available for a particular database, C<Database::Join> plays it
 1550: safe and uses the array path for the whole query without fetching any rows.
 1551: 
 1552: =back
 1553: 
 1554: B<Choosing max_array_rows>
 1555: 
 1556: The default of 10,000 is a reasonable starting point.  Adjust it to match
 1557: your hardware and typical row width.  For wide rows (many columns or long
 1558: strings) you may want a lower threshold; for narrow rows you can raise it.
 1559: 
 1560: B<Temporary file: name, location, and lifetime>
 1561: 
 1562: When the SQLite path is active, a single temporary SQLite database file acts
 1563: as the join cache for the life of the C<Database::Join> object.
 1564: 
 1565: B<Name>: the filename is randomly generated by C<File::Temp>, for example:
 1566: 
 1567:     /tmp/Cj8xK7mP2Q.db
 1568: 
 1569: The random portion (ten characters) is chosen automatically to avoid
 1570: collisions.  Only the directory is under your control; you cannot specify
 1571: the filename itself.
 1572: 
 1573: B<Location>: controlled by the C<tmpdir> constructor parameter.
 1574: If C<tmpdir> is not specified, C<File::Spec-E<gt>tmpdir()> is used (usually
 1575: C</tmp> on Unix, or the value of the C<TEMP> or C<TMP> environment variable
 1576: on Windows).
 1577: 
 1578: B<Lifetime: one file per object, deleted when the object is destroyed>: the
 1579: file is created on the first query call that uses the SQLite path and kept
 1580: alive until the C<Database::Join> object is destroyed (i.e. when it goes out
 1581: of scope or is explicitly C<undef>-d).  At most one file exists per object at
 1582: any given moment.  Calling C<selectall_arrayref()> ten times on the same
 1583: object creates and uses I<one> file, not ten.
 1584: 
 1585: B<Cache invalidation>: the cache is automatically rebuilt (the old file is
 1586: replaced with a new one) when any source database's C<updated()> return value
 1587: changes, or when C<add_database()> is called.  Base-filter criteria
 1588: (C<filters> constructor parameter) are applied once at build time for spilled
 1589: sources; query-time criteria are applied per-call as SQL C<WHERE> clauses.
 1590: 
 1591: To use a different directory -- for example a RAM-backed filesystem or a
 1592: faster local disk:
 1593: 
 1594:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1595:         databases      => [ $db1, $db2 ],
 1596:         join_column    => 'entry',
 1597:         backend        => 'sqlite',
 1598:         tmpdir         => '/dev/shm',     # Linux RAM disk
 1599:     );
 1600: 
 1601: B<Parallel secondary fetches (C<parallel> constructor parameter)>
 1602: 
 1603: By default, component databases are queried sequentially - the primary first,
 1604: then each secondary in order.  When the component databases are network- or
 1605: disk-backed and have non-trivial per-query latency, the sequential fetch means
 1606: total latency is the I<sum> of all per-DA latencies.
 1607: 
 1608: Setting C<< parallel => 1 >> in the constructor enables concurrent fetching
 1609: of secondary databases using Perl C<threads>.  With C<parallel => 1> and
 1610: two or more secondary databases (C<n E<gt> 2> total), secondary fetches run in
 1611: parallel after the primary fetch completes; total latency drops to
 1612: I<max(secondary latencies)> instead of I<sum(secondary latencies)>.
 1613: 
 1614:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1615:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty, $scores ],   # 3 DAs - 2 secondaries
 1616:         join_column => 'entry',
 1617:         parallel    => 1,    # loyalty and scores fetched concurrently
 1618:     );
 1619: 
 1620: Requirements and caveats:
 1621: 
 1622: =over 4
 1623: 
 1624: =item *
 1625: 
 1626: The C<threads> module must be available.  Most distributions ship it, but it
 1627: requires a Perl binary compiled with C<-Dusethreads>.  When threads are
 1628: unavailable, a C<carp> warning is emitted and fetching falls back to
 1629: sequential; the result is identical, only slower.
 1630: 
 1631: =item *
 1632: 
 1633: Parallel fetching is only active when the join has three or more total
 1634: databases (C<n E<gt> 2>).  With two databases (one secondary), the thread
 1635: creation overhead exceeds the benefit of concurrency; sequential is used
 1636: regardless of C<parallel>.
 1637: 
 1638: =item *
 1639: 
 1640: Component databases must be safe to call from Perl threads.  In-memory
 1641: databases (CSV, JSON, TSV after slurp) are safe.  DBI-backed databases
 1642: whose handles were created in the same thread may not be safe - consult your
 1643: DBD driver's thread documentation.  The array backend is recommended for
 1644: DBI-backed sources; the SQLite backend performs its join in a single SQL
 1645: statement and does not use parallel fetching.
 1646: 
 1647: =item *
 1648: 
 1649: C<parallel => 1> applies to the array backend only.  The SQLite backend
 1650: performs a single SQL JOIN after spilling source data, so per-DA parallelism
 1651: is irrelevant.
 1652: 
 1653: =back
 1654: 
 1655: B<Zero-copy ATTACH (C<dbi_source()> interface)>
 1656: 
 1657: Normally, when the SQLite path is active, rows from each component database
 1658: are fetched one by one and inserted into the temporary SQLite file.  This is
 1659: efficient but does involve INSERT overhead.
 1660: 
 1661: If a component database is itself SQLite-backed and implements a
 1662: C<dbi_source()> method, C<Database::Join> can skip the row-by-row copy
 1663: entirely and instead use C<ATTACH DATABASE> to link the source file directly
 1664: to the temporary join connection.  This is the zero-copy path and is
 1665: significantly faster for large SQLite sources.
 1666: 
 1667: The C<dbi_source()> method must return a hashref with two keys:
 1668: 
 1669: =over 4
 1670: 
 1671: =item C<dbh>
 1672: 
 1673: A connected C<DBD::SQLite> database handle (C<DBI> connection object).
 1674: 
 1675: =item C<table>
 1676: 
 1677: The name of the table in that database that holds the source rows.
 1678: 
 1679: =back
 1680: 
 1681: Example implementation:
 1682: 
 1683:     package My::SQLiteDatabase;
 1684:     use parent 'Database::Abstraction';
 1685: 
 1686:     sub dbi_source {
 1687:         my ($self) = @_;
 1688:         return {
 1689:             dbh   => $self->{_dbh},      # connected DBD::SQLite handle
 1690:             table => $self->{_table},    # table name in that database
 1691:         };
 1692:     }
 1693: 
 1694:     1;
 1695: 
 1696: The zero-copy ATTACH path is always used when a component database implements
 1697: C<dbi_source()>.  Query-time criteria are applied as parameterised SQL
 1698: C<WHERE> clauses against the ATTACHed source table, so no row-level copy is
 1699: needed even when the current call includes column filters.  (Prior to 0.005.0
 1700: any query-time criteria forced a spill; that restriction was removed in
 1701: 0.005.0.)
 1702: 
 1703: B<Result identity>
 1704: 
 1705: Both the array path and the SQLite path produce identical results for any
 1706: given query.  You can switch between them freely without changing callers.
 1707: The C<collision_prefix> column renaming, C<join_map> key translation, and all
 1708: three join types (left, inner, outer) work identically on both paths.
 1709: 
 1710: =head2 selectall_arrayref
 1711: 
 1712: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 1713: 
 1714:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1715:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier  => 'gold');
 1716:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(score => { '>' => 80 });
 1717:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref('C001');  # positional: entry => 'C001'
 1718:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(sort_by => 'name');
 1719:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold', sort_by => ['score', 'DESC']);
 1720:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(limit => 10);
 1721:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(limit => 10, offset => 20);
 1722:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold', sort_by => 'name', limit => 5);
 1723:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(distinct => 1, columns => ['tier']);
 1724:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(distinct => 1);  # distinct on all columns
 1725: 
 1726: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 1727: 
 1728: Returns an arrayref of hashrefs representing the merged view of all component
 1729: databases, optionally filtered by the given criteria.
 1730: 
 1731: Criteria for columns that live in different databases are routed
 1732: automatically: each database is queried with only the criteria that apply to
 1733: its own columns.  The results are combined in memory using C<join_column>.
 1734: Under a C<left> or C<inner> join, a secondary database is also limited to the
 1735: primary's keys (see "Key pushdown" in L</DESCRIPTION>).
 1736: 
 1737: Accepts the same criteria syntax as C<Database::Abstraction::selectall_arrayref>.
 1738: A single plain scalar argument is interpreted as the C<join_column> value
 1739: (equivalent to C<< entry => 'C001' >> when C<join_column> is C<'entry'>).
 1740: 
 1741: Pass C<< distinct => 1 >> (with optional C<< columns => \@cols >>) to
 1742: deduplicate results; see the C<distinct> feature section for details.
 1743: 
 1744: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 1745: 
 1746: =head4 Input
 1747: 
 1748:     Calling conventions (in order of precedence):
 1749:       1. No arguments             -- returns all rows
 1750:       2. One plain scalar         -- shorthand for join_column => $scalar
 1751:       3. Key-value pairs or
 1752:          a criteria hashref       -- routed per-database
 1753: 
 1754:     Values may be:
 1755:       Plain scalar                -- exact match
 1756:       Hashref of operators        -- e.g. { '>' => 80 }
 1757: 
 1758:     Optional parameters (mixed in with any of the above):
 1759:       sort_by => 'colname'            -- sort ascending by that column
 1760:       sort_by => ['colname', 'DESC']  -- sort descending
 1761:       sort_by => ['colname', 'ASC']   -- sort ascending (explicit)
 1762:       limit    => N                    -- return at most N rows (positive integer)
 1763:       offset   => M                   -- skip the first M rows (non-negative integer)
 1764: 
 1765:     The column named in sort_by must be present in the merged view (i.e. it
 1766:     must appear in columns()).  An unknown column or an invalid direction emits
 1767:     a carp warning and falls back to the default join_column ascending sort.
 1768: 
 1769:     limit and offset are applied after ordering.  offset without limit skips
 1770:     rows but returns all remaining rows.  limit without offset starts from
 1771:     the first qualifying row.  An invalid limit or offset emits a carp warning
 1772:     and the parameter is ignored (treated as absent).
 1773: 
 1774:     DOMAIN -- sort_by:
 1775:       EP absent:          result sorted by join_column ASC (default).
 1776:       EP string:          any column name in columns(); sorts ASC by that column.
 1777:       EP ['col','ASC']:   explicit ascending; equivalent to the string form.
 1778:       EP ['col','DESC']:  descending sort by the named column.
 1779:       EP ['col']:         single-element arrayref; direction defaults to ASC.
 1780:       EP []:              empty arrayref; column is undef -> carp + join_col ASC fallback.
 1781:       EP invalid column:  column not in columns() -> carp + join_col ASC fallback.
 1782:       EP invalid dir:     direction not 'ASC' or 'DESC' -> carp + ASC used.
 1783:       Sort is lexicographic (cmp); use backend=>'sqlite' for numeric ORDER BY.
 1784: 
 1785:     DOMAIN -- limit:
 1786:       EP absent:          no truncation; all qualifying rows are returned.
 1787:       EP 0:               not a positive integer -> carp + ignored (all rows returned).
 1788:       BVA min valid = 1:  exactly 1 row returned.
 1789:       BVA at count:       limit == total rows -> all rows returned (no truncation).
 1790:       BVA above count:    limit > total rows -> all rows returned.
 1791:       EP invalid:         negative integer, float string, or non-numeric string
 1792:                           => carp + ignored (all rows returned).
 1793:       Valid domain:       integers in [1, INF); matched by /^\d+\z/a with value >= 1.
 1794: 
 1795:     DOMAIN -- offset:
 1796:       EP absent:          no rows skipped; result starts from row 0.
 1797:       BVA min valid = 0:  no rows skipped (zero is a valid non-negative integer).
 1798:       BVA offset=1:       first row skipped; result starts from row 1.
 1799:       BVA offset=N-1:     N-1 rows skipped; only the last row returned.
 1800:       BVA offset=N:       all N rows skipped; empty result returned.
 1801:       BVA offset>N:       all rows skipped; empty result returned.
 1802:       EP invalid:         negative integer, float string, or non-numeric string
 1803:                           => carp + ignored (no rows skipped).
 1804:       Valid domain:       integers in [0, INF); matched by /^\d+\z/a.
 1805: 
 1806:     distinct => 1          -- deduplicate rows after the merge (see columns below)
 1807:     columns  => \@cols     -- arrayref of column names to project and deduplicate on;
 1808:                               used together with distinct => 1
 1809: 
 1810:     DOMAIN -- distinct:
 1811:       EP absent or 0:     no deduplication; all merged rows are returned.
 1812:       EP 1:               duplicates are removed (see below); first occurrence wins.
 1813:       EP truthy (not 1):  treated as truthy; deduplication is applied.
 1814:       Effect:             on the array backend, a Perl seen-hash keyed on the
 1815:                           selected column values is used; first occurrence wins.
 1816:                           On the SQLite backend, SELECT DISTINCT is used.
 1817: 
 1818:     DOMAIN -- columns:
 1819:       EP absent/undef:    when distinct is set, deduplication is on all columns
 1820:                           and full rows are returned.
 1821:       EP []:              empty arrayref treated the same as absent.
 1822:       EP valid:           arrayref of column names present in the merged view;
 1823:                           result rows are projected to those columns only, and
 1824:                           deduplication is on the projected values.
 1825:       EP with distinct=0: columns is ignored when distinct is absent or false.
 1826:       Note:               unknown column names in the list are silently ignored
 1827:                           at projection time; the row may lack those keys.
 1828: 
 1829: =head4 Output
 1830: 
 1831:     Arrayref of hashrefs; one hashref per qualifying merged row.
 1832:     Sorted ascending by join_column by default; caller-controlled via sort_by.
 1833:     At most C<limit> rows when limit is given; the first C<offset> rows are
 1834:     skipped when offset is given.
 1835:     Returns a reference to an empty array when no rows match.
 1836: 
 1837: =head3 EXAMPLE
 1838: 
 1839:     # All rows from both databases
 1840:     my $all = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1841: 
 1842:     # Only rows where the 'tier' column (from the loyalty database)
 1843:     # equals 'gold' -- the criterion is routed to the right database
 1844:     my $vip = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold');
 1845: 
 1846:     # Operator hashref: score > 80
 1847:     my $high = $join->selectall_arrayref(score => { '>' => 80 });
 1848: 
 1849:     # Access each merged row
 1850:     for my $row (@{$vip}) {
 1851:         printf "%-10s tier=%-8s score=%d\n",
 1852:             $row->{entry}, $row->{tier}, $row->{score} // 0;
 1853:     }
 1854: 
 1855:     # Distinct tier values across all rows (project to one column, deduplicate)
 1856:     my $tiers = $join->selectall_arrayref(distinct => 1, columns => ['tier']);
 1857:     # Returns e.g. [{ tier => 'gold' }, { tier => 'silver' }, { tier => 'bronze' }]
 1858:     # regardless of how many rows exist for each tier
 1859: 
 1860:     # Distinct region+tier combinations
 1861:     my $combos = $join->selectall_arrayref(
 1862:         distinct => 1,
 1863:         columns  => ['region', 'tier'],
 1864:     );

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 1865: 
 1866:     # Distinct on all columns (remove fully-duplicate merged rows)
 1867:     my $deduped = $join->selectall_arrayref(distinct => 1);
 1868: 
 1869: =head3 MESSAGES
 1870: 
 1871:     warn_unknown_column (carp)
 1872:         -- A criterion key names a column not present in any component database;
 1873:            the criterion is silently dropped and all rows are returned.
 1874:     error_join_criterion (croak)
 1875:         -- A criterion hashref contains a join => key (DA SQL-JOIN syntax).
 1876:            Use a separate DA object for the joined table instead.
 1877:     sort_by column unknown (carp)
 1878:         -- The column given in sort_by is not in the merged view; the result
 1879:            is returned in the default join_column ascending order instead.
 1880:     sort_by direction invalid (carp)
 1881:         -- The direction given in sort_by is not 'ASC' or 'DESC'; ASC is used.
 1882:     limit invalid (carp)
 1883:         -- The value given for limit is not a positive integer; it is ignored.
 1884:     offset invalid (carp)
 1885:         -- The value given for offset is not a non-negative integer; it is ignored.
 1886:     operator-unsupported (carp, SQLite/auto path only)
 1887:         -- An operator hashref key is not in the supported set (>, <, >=, <=,
 1888:            !=, =, LIKE, NOT LIKE, IS NULL, IS NOT NULL, IN, NOT IN); the
 1889:            individual operator term is dropped from the WHERE clause (other
 1890:            operators in the same hashref still apply).
 1891:     IN/NOT IN wrong value type (carp, SQLite/auto path only)
 1892:         -- An IN or NOT IN criterion was given a non-arrayref value; the operator
 1893:            term is skipped.
 1894:     error_sqlite_connect (croak, SQLite/auto path only)
 1895:         -- The temporary SQLite join file could not be created; check tmpdir
 1896:            permissions and available disk space.
 1897: 
 1898: =cut
 1899: 
 1900: sub selectall_arrayref {
 1901: 	my ($self, @args) = @_;
 1902: 	my $params   = $self->_parse_query_args(undef, @args);
 1903: 	my $sort_by  = delete $params->{sort_by};
 1904: 	my $limit    = delete $params->{limit};
 1905: 	my $offset   = delete $params->{offset};
 1906: 	my $distinct = delete $params->{distinct};
 1907: 	my $columns  = delete $params->{columns};
 1908: 	return $self->_joined_query($params,
 1909: 		sort_by  => $sort_by, limit => $limit, offset => $offset,
 1910: 		distinct => $distinct, columns => $columns);
 1911: }
 1912: 
 1913: =head2 selectall_array
 1914: 
 1915: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 1916: 
 1917:     my @rows = $join->selectall_array(tier => 'gold');
 1918: 
 1919:     # Scalar context: only the first matching row
 1920:     my $first = $join->selectall_array(entry => 'C001');
 1921: 
 1922:     # Distinct column projection (list context)
 1923:     my @tiers = $join->selectall_array(distinct => 1, columns => ['tier']);
 1924: 
 1925:     # Distinct on all columns
 1926:     my @deduped = $join->selectall_array(distinct => 1);
 1927: 
 1928: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 1929: 
 1930: In list context returns a list of merged hashrefs -- the same rows that

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 1931: C<selectall_arrayref> would return, just as a flat list rather than an
 1932: arrayref.
 1933: 
 1934: In scalar context returns only the first matching hashref (or C<undef> if
 1935: nothing matches).
 1936: 
 1937: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 1938: 
 1939: =head4 Input
 1940: 
 1941:     Same as selectall_arrayref.
 1942: 
 1943: =head4 Output
 1944: 

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 1945:     List context:   list of hashrefs (may be empty).
 1946:     Scalar context: single hashref or undef.
 1947: 
 1948: =head3 EXAMPLE
 1949: 
 1950:     my @all = $join->selectall_array();
 1951:     print scalar @all, " rows\n";
 1952: 
 1953:     # First gold-tier customer only
 1954:     my $first_vip = $join->selectall_array(tier => 'gold');
 1955:     print $first_vip->{name}, "\n" if defined $first_vip;
 1956: 
 1957: =head3 MESSAGES
 1958: 

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 1959: Same messages as C<selectall_arrayref>.
 1960: 
 1961: =cut

 2071: query is executed against the cached join tables, avoiding a full row fetch.

 2098: sub count {
 2099: 	my ($self, @args) = @_;
 2100: 	my $params = $self->_parse_query_args(undef, @args);
 2101: 	delete $params->{sort_by};  # row ordering is irrelevant for a count
 2102: 	delete $params->{limit};     # count returns total matching rows, not a page
 2103: 	delete $params->{offset};
 2104: 	# On the SQLite path, push COUNT(*) into SQL to avoid fetching all rows.
 2105: 	return $self->_sqlite_join($params, count_only => 1)
 2106: 		unless $self->{_backend} eq 'array';
 2107: 	return scalar @{ $self->_joined_query_array($params) };

 2110: =head2 aggregate
 2111: 
 2112: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2113: 
 2114:     my $rows = $join->aggregate(
 2115:         by       => ['tier'],
 2116:         measures => [
 2117:             { col => 'score', fn => 'sum', as => 'total' },
 2118:             {                 fn => 'count', as => 'n'   },
 2119:         ],
 2120:         having   => { total => { '>' => 100 } },
 2121:         sort_by  => ['total', 'DESC'],
 2122:         limit    => 10,
 2123:     );
 2124:     # => [ { tier => 'gold', total => 183, n => 2 }, ... ]
 2125: 
 2126: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2127: 
 2128: Groups the merged view by one or more published column names and computes
 2129: aggregate measures over each group.
 2130: 
 2131: On the SQLite backend (C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'>) and the C<auto> backend,
 2132: the aggregation is pushed into the existing SQLite cache as a C<GROUP BY>
 2133: query - rows never pass through Perl.  The C<auto> backend always takes this
 2134: path for aggregation, skipping the row-count threshold probe (aggregation
 2135: makes the row count irrelevant).
 2136: 
 2137: On the array backend (C<backend =E<gt> 'array'>), merged rows are fetched in
 2138: full and folded in Perl.  This materialises the complete matching result set.
 2139: 
 2140: The two paths return the same rows for the same input, except for two
 2141: documented differences: C<min>/C<max> on a column declared C<TEXT> but
 2142: storing numbers (SQL compares lexically; Perl detects numerics), and
 2143: C<sum>/C<avg> with non-numeric values (SQL coerces to 0.0; Perl skips and
 2144: warns via C<warn_aggregate_non_numeric>).
 2145: 
 2146: C<Database::Join> has no C<no_fixate> and no CHI cache (unlike
 2147: C<Database::Abstraction>); aggregation results are not cached between calls.
 2148: 
 2149: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2150: 
 2151: =head4 Input
 2152: 
 2153: Reserved keys extracted before the rest is treated as WHERE criteria:
 2154: 
 2155:     by       => arrayref of published column names (default: [] = grand total)
 2156:     measures => arrayref of measure hashrefs (required, non-empty)
 2157:     having   => hashref of post-aggregation criteria
 2158:     sort_by  => 'col' or ['col', 'DESC']
 2159:     limit    => positive integer
 2160:     offset   => non-negative integer
 2161: 
 2162: Every other key is pre-aggregation criteria routed exactly as
 2163: C<selectall_arrayref> routes criteria.
 2164: 
 2165: Measure hashref keys:
 2166: 
 2167:     fn  => function name (sum|avg|min|max|count|count_distinct), case-insensitive
 2168:     col => published column name (required for all functions except bare count)
 2169:     as  => result key (required; must match C</^\w+\z/>; unique; not a by name)
 2170: 
 2171: C<having> uses the same operator vocabulary as C<selectall_arrayref>:
 2172: C<%SAFE_SQL_OPS> (E<gt>, E<lt>, E<gt>=, E<lt>=, !=, =, LIKE, NOT LIKE),
 2173: C<%SAFE_LIST_OPS> (IN, NOT IN), C<%SAFE_NOARG_OPS> (IS NULL, IS NOT NULL),
 2174: a bare scalar (= ?), and C<-or>/C<-and> groupings.  Write C<-between> as
 2175: two operators and C<LIKE> explicitly.
 2176: 
 2177: =head4 Output
 2178: 
 2179: Always an arrayref of hashrefs (never C<undef>).  C<[]> when no group
 2180: qualifies and C<by> is non-empty.  When C<by> is omitted or C<[]> and no
 2181: rows match, returns one all-normalised row (grand total of zero).
 2182: 
 2183: Key order per row: C<by> columns in declaration order, then measure aliases
 2184: in declaration order.
 2185: 
 2186: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2187: 
 2188:     # Total score and row count per tier, highest total first
 2189:     my $rows = $join->aggregate(
 2190:         by       => ['tier'],
 2191:         measures => [
 2192:             { col => 'score', fn => 'sum',   as => 'total' },
 2193:             {                 fn => 'count', as => 'n'     },
 2194:         ],
 2195:         sort_by  => ['total', 'DESC'],
 2196:     );

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
 2197:     for my $r (@{$rows}) {
 2198:         printf "%s: total=%s n=%s\n", $r->{tier}, $r->{total}, $r->{n};
 2199:     }
 2200: 
 2201: =head3 PSEUDOCODE
 2202: 
 2203:     1. get_params(undef, @args) -- hashref or kv-pairs only (no positional key)

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
 2204:     2. croak error_join_criterion if 'join =>' present
 2205:     3. _validate_aggregate_spec extracts by/measures/having/sort_by/limit/offset
 2206:     4. array backend:
 2207:          rows   = _joined_query_array(remaining criteria, no sort/limit/offset)
 2208:          groups = _fold_aggregate(rows, spec)
 2209:          groups = [grep { _match_criteria($_, having) } @groups] if having

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 2210:          _normalise_aggregate(groups, spec.measures)
 2211:          _sort_and_page(groups, spec)
 2212:        sqlite/auto backend:

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 2213:          _sqlite_aggregate(remaining criteria, spec)
 2214:     5. return groups
 2215: 
 2216: =head3 MESSAGES

 2260: =head2 each_row
 2261: 
 2262: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2263: 
 2264:     my $count = $join->each_row(sub { my ($row) = @_; ... });
 2265:     my $count = $join->each_row(sub { ... }, tier => 'gold');
 2266:     my $count = $join->each_row(sub { ... }, sort_by => 'name', limit => 100);
 2267: 
 2268: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2269: 
 2270: Calls C<\&callback> once for every row in the merged view that matches the
 2271: given criteria, then returns the total count of rows visited.
 2272: 
 2273: Accepts the same criteria, C<sort_by>, C<limit>, and C<offset> parameters as
 2274: C<selectall_arrayref>.
 2275: 
 2276: B<Note on memory usage>: C<Database::Join> always materialises the complete
 2277: merged result set before invoking the callback, because merging rows from
 2278: multiple independent sources requires that every source be queried and
 2279: cross-referenced first.  True constant-memory streaming (as C<Database::Abstraction>
 2280: provides on single-source SQL queries) is not possible at the join layer.
 2281: For genuinely large result sets use the SQLite backend (C<backend =E<gt>
 2282: 'sqlite'>), which keeps peak RAM to approximately one times the source data
 2283: size rather than three.
 2284: 
 2285: Any exception thrown inside the callback propagates to the caller after the
 2286: current row; subsequent rows are not visited.
 2287: 
 2288: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2289: 
 2290: =head4 Input
 2291: 
 2292:     \&callback   Positional coderef (required).
 2293:                  Called as $callback->($row_hashref) for each merged row.

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 2294: 
 2295:     Additional arguments follow the same calling conventions as
 2296:     selectall_arrayref: no args, a single scalar join-column value,
 2297:     or key-value criteria pairs (including sort_by, limit, offset).
 2298: 
 2299: =head4 Output
 2300: 
 2301:     Non-negative integer: the count of rows for which the callback was invoked.
 2302: 
 2303: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2304: 
 2305:     # Print every gold-tier customer's name, sorted by name
 2306:     my $count = $join->each_row(
 2307:         sub { my ($row) = @_; print "$row->{name}\n" },
 2308:         tier    => 'gold',
 2309:         sort_by => 'name',
 2310:     );
 2311:     print "$count gold-tier customers\n";
 2312: 
 2313:     # Accumulate without holding the full result
 2314:     my $total_score = 0;
 2315:     $join->each_row(sub { $total_score += $_[0]->{score} // 0 });
 2316: 
 2317: =head3 MESSAGES
 2318: 
 2319:     error_invalid_callback (croak)
 2320:         -- First argument is not a code reference.
 2321: 
 2322: =cut
 2323: 
 2324: sub each_row {
●2325 → 2333 → 2337 2325: 	my ($self, $cb, @args) = @_;
 2326: 	croak $self->_err('error_invalid_callback')
 2327: 		unless ref($cb) eq 'CODE';
 2328: 	# Materialise the full merged result first; multi-source joins cannot
 2329: 	# stream rows one at a time because the merge key set is not known until
 2330: 	# all sources have been queried and cross-referenced.
 2331: 	my $rows  = $self->selectall_arrayref(@args);
 2332: 	my $count = 0;
 2333: 	for my $row (@{$rows}) {
 2334: 		$cb->($row);
 2335: 		$count++;
 2336: 	}
 2337: 	return $count;
 2338: }

 2340: =head2 dbi_source
 2341: 
 2342: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2343: 
 2344:     # Use a Database::Join object as a zero-copy SQLite source inside a
 2345:     # parent Database::Join, giving the parent ATTACHed-speed access to
 2346:     # the child's joined data without iterating through Perl.
 2347:     my $child  = Database::Join->new(databases => [$da, $db], join_column => 'id', backend => 'sqlite');
 2348:     my $parent = Database::Join->new(databases => [$child, $dc], join_column => 'id', backend => 'sqlite');
 2349: 
 2350: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2351: 
 2352: Returns a hashref C<< { dbh => $sqlite_dbh, table => '_dj_result' } >> that
 2353: allows a parent C<Database::Join> (or any other caller that understands the
 2354: C<dbi_source()> interface) to ATTACH the child's temporary SQLite database
 2355: file and query the materialised join result directly via SQL, without routing
 2356: rows through Perl.
 2357: 
 2358: The first call builds the SQLite cache (if not already current) and
 2359: materialises the full join result - with C<filters> applied but no query-time
 2360: criteria - into a real table named C<_dj_result> inside the cache file.
 2361: Subsequent calls within the same cache cycle reuse the existing table.
 2362: 
 2363: Returns C<undef> when the backend is C<'array'> (no SQLite file exists).

 2374:       Hashref with keys:
 2375:         dbh   => DBI handle to the child's temporary SQLite database file.

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
 2376:         table => '_dj_result'  (the materialized join table inside that file).
 2377:     On the array backend:
 2378:       undef
 2379: 
 2380: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2381: 
 2382:     # The parent automatically ATTACHes the child's SQLite file and queries
 2383:     # _dj_result for zero-copy composable nested joins.

 2403: sub dbi_source {
●2404 → 2419 → 2427 2404: 	my ($self) = @_;
 2405: 
 2406: 	# The array backend has no SQLite handle to expose.
 2407: 	return undef if $self->{_backend} eq 'array';
 2408: 
 2409: 	# Build or refresh the per-source SQLite cache.  On 'auto' backend this
 2410: 	# forces the SQLite path regardless of the auto-threshold decision â€” a
 2411: 	# parent that wants to ATTACH us requires a real on-disk file.
 2412: 	$self->_build_sqlite_cache() unless $self->_cache_fresh();

 2414: 	my $cache = $self->{_sqlite_cache};
 2415: 
 2416: 	# If the materialised result table is already current, reuse it.
 2417: 	# _dj_built is reset implicitly when _build_sqlite_cache creates a fresh
 2418: 	# cache hashref (the old hashref is replaced, so its _dj_built is gone).
 2419: 	unless ($cache->{_dj_built}) {

 2421: 		# criteria) into _dj_result so a parent connection can ATTACH and
 2422: 		# query it as a plain table.
 2423: 		$self->_sqlite_join({}, create_table => '_dj_result');
 2424: 		$cache->{_dj_built} = 1;
 2425: 	}
 2426: 
 2427: 	return { dbh => $cache->{dbh}, table => '_dj_result' };
 2428: }

 2543: sub schema {
●2544 → 2552 → 2568 2544: 	my ($self) = @_;
 2545: 
 2546: 	return $self->{_schema_cache} if $self->{_schema_cache};
 2547: 
 2548: 	# Hash slice assignment (@merged{keys} = values) is O(M) per database;
 2549: 	# the previous (%merged = (%merged, %s)) pattern was O(NÃ—M) per iteration,
 2550: 	# totalling O(NÂ²Ã—M) over all databases for the same result.
 2551: 	my %merged;
 2552: 	for my $i (0 .. $#{ $self->{_dbs} }) {
 2553: 		my $s        = $self->{_dbs}[$i]->schema() // {};
 2554: 		my $local_jc = $self->{_join_map}{$i};
 2555: 		my $renames  = $self->{_col_rename}[$i] // {};
 2556: 		my $skip_jc  = $local_jc && $local_jc ne $self->{_join_col};
 2557: 		if (!$skip_jc && !%{$renames}) {
 2558: 			# Fast path: no join-key alias to exclude and no collision renames
 2559: 			@merged{keys %{$s}} = values %{$s};
 2560: 		} else {
 2561: 			for my $col (keys %{$s}) {
 2562: 				next if $skip_jc && $col eq $local_jc;
 2563: 				$merged{ $renames->{$col} // $col } = $s->{$col};
 2564: 			}
 2565: 		}
 2566: 	}
 2567: 
 2568: 	delete @merged{keys %{ $self->{_removed_cols} }}
 2569: 		if %{ $self->{_removed_cols} };
 2570: 
 2571: 	$self->{_schema_cache} = \%merged;
 2572: 	return $self->{_schema_cache};
 2573: }

 2575: =head2 updated
 2576: 
 2577: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2578: 
 2579:     my $ts = $join->updated();
 2580: 
 2581: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2582: 

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 2583: Returns the Unix timestamp of the most recent modification across all
 2584: component databases.  This is the maximum of all individual C<updated()>
 2585: return values.
 2586: 
 2587: Use this to implement simple cache-invalidation logic: if C<updated()>
 2588: has advanced since your last snapshot, re-query.
 2589: 
 2590: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2591: 
 2592: =head4 Input
 2593: 
 2594:     None.
 2595: 
 2596: =head4 Output
 2597: 
 2598:     Unix timestamp (positive integer).
 2599: 
 2600: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2601: 
 2602:     my $last_modified = $join->updated();
 2603:     if ($last_modified > $my_cache_timestamp) {
 2604:         $my_cache = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 2605:         $my_cache_timestamp = $last_modified;
 2606:     }
 2607: 
 2608: =head3 MESSAGES
 2609: 
 2610: C<updated()> does not emit any warnings or errors.  Component databases that do
 2611: not implement C<updated()>, or whose C<updated()> throws, are silently skipped;
 2612: only defined return values contribute to the maximum.  If no component database
 2613: implements C<updated()>, C<undef> is returned (same as C<List::Util::max> on an
 2614: empty list).
 2615: 
 2616: =cut
 2617: 
 2618: sub updated {
●2619 → 2621 → 2626 2619: 	my ($self) = @_;
 2620: 	my @timestamps;
 2621: 	for my $db (@{ $self->{_dbs} }) {
 2622: 		my $ts;
 2623: 		do { local $@; $ts = eval { $db->updated() } };
 2624: 		push @timestamps, $ts if defined $ts;
 2625: 	}
 2626: 	return max(@timestamps);
 2627: }

 2629: =head2 set_logger
 2630:

 2632:

 2634:     $join->set_logger(logger => $log);   # named-pair form also accepted
 2635: 
 2636: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2637:

 2639: database.  The logger is used for diagnostic output by all component databases.
 2640: 
 2641: Non-blessed values (a log-level string such as C<"debug">, a filename, or a
 2642: code reference) are wrapped in C<Log::Abstraction->new(...)> automatically,
 2643: matching the behaviour of C<Database::Abstraction::set_logger>.
 2644: 
 2645: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2646: 
 2647: =head4 Input
 2648: 
 2649:     logger  Positional or named: a logger object, log-level string, filename,

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 2650:             or code reference (required).  Non-blessed values are wrapped in
 2651:             Log::Abstraction automatically.
 2652: 
 2653: =head4 Output
 2654: 
 2655:     Returns C<$self> for method chaining.
 2656: 
 2657: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2658: 
 2659:     # Log::Any is used here as an example; any object that implements
 2660:     # debug() and info() (or whichever methods your component databases
 2661:     # call internally) works equally well.
 2662:     use Log::Any qw($log);
 2663: 
 2664:     my $join = Database::Join->new(databases => [$db1, $db2], join_column => 'entry');
 2665:     $join->set_logger($log);
 2666:     # $log is now used by $join and by $db1 and $db2
 2667: 
 2668:     # Named-pair form (mirrors Database::Abstraction API):

 2670: 
 2671: =head3 MESSAGES

 2674:         -- Called with an undefined argument.  Pass a valid logger object.
 2675:

 2684:

 2696: =head2 add_database
 2697: 
 2698: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2699: 
 2700:     # Positional: database object as first argument
 2701:     $join->add_database($db);
 2702: 
 2703:     # Named: equivalent to the above
 2704:     $join->add_database(database => $db);
 2705: 
 2706:     # With options (mixed positional + named)
 2707:     $join->add_database($db, remove_columns => ['internal_id']);
 2708:     $join->add_database($db, join_column    => 'local_key_name');
 2709:     $join->add_database($db, filter         => { score => { '>' => 60 } });
 2710: 
 2711:     # Chainable
 2712:     $join->add_database($db1)->add_database($db2, remove_columns => ['notes']);
 2713: 
 2714: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2715: 
 2716: Adds one more C<Database::Abstraction> subclass object to the logical view
 2717: and immediately updates the column-ownership index.
 2718: 
 2719: After the call, all query methods return rows that include columns from the
 2720: newly added database, and criteria on those new columns are routed to it
 2721: automatically.
 2722: 
 2723: When a column name in the new database already exists in an earlier database,
 2724: the new database becomes the authoritative source for that column
 2725: (last-database-wins, the same rule that applies at construction time).
 2726: 
 2727: The join-column must be present in the new database (or declared via
 2728: C<join_column>).  The logger is propagated to the new database if one is set.
 2729: 
 2730: C<add_database> is the runtime equivalent of listing the database in the
 2731: C<databases> array to C<new>.  The optional C<join_column> parameter is
 2732: equivalent to a C<join_map> entry; the optional C<filter> parameter is
 2733: equivalent to a C<filters> entry.
 2734: 
 2735: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2736: 
 2737: =head4 Input
 2738: 
 2739:     database       => { type => 'object',   required => 1 }
 2740:                       # A Database::Abstraction subclass instance.
 2741:                       #
 2742:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   blessed object that passes
 2743:                       #                       isa('Database::Abstraction').
 2744:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: non-reference, unblessed ref, wrong class,
 2745:                       #                       or non-reference non-key scalar (the guard at
 2746:                       #                       the top of add_database rejects it with
 2747:                       #                       error_invalid_db before validate_strict runs).
 2748: 
 2749:     join_column    => { type => 'string',   optional => 1 }
 2750:                       # The name of the join key in THIS new database,
 2751:                       # when it differs from the canonical join_column.
 2752:                       #
 2753:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any string that exists as a column in the
 2754:                       #                       new database.
 2755:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: string absent from the new database's columns()
 2756:                       #                       => croak error_join_col_missing.
 2757: 
 2758:     filter         => { type => 'hashref',  optional => 1 }
 2759:                       # Permanent criteria for this database only.
 2760:                       # Same format as selectall_arrayref.
 2761:                       #
 2762:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   hashref of criteria (may be {} for no-op).
 2763:                       # DOMAIN -- EP absent:  no permanent filter applied; all rows visible.
 2764:                       # DOMAIN -- Key-set:    a non-empty filter makes this DB an inner-join
 2765:                       #                       partner regardless of the outer join_type.
 2766: 
 2767:     remove_columns => { type => 'arrayref', optional => 1 }
 2768:                       # Column names from this database to hide.
 2769:                       #
 2770:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   arrayref of strings; non-existent columns silently
 2771:                       #                       ignored; empty [] is a safe no-op.
 2772:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: join_column itself => croak error_remove_join_col.
 2773: 
 2774: =head4 Output
 2775: 
 2776:     Returns C<$self> to support method chaining.
 2777: 
 2778: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2779: 
 2780:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 2781:         databases   => [ $customers ],
 2782:         join_column => 'entry',
 2783:     );
 2784: 
 2785:     # Add loyalty data; hide internal columns from it
 2786:     $join->add_database($loyalty, remove_columns => ['audit_ts']);
 2787: 
 2788:     # Add score data; only include rows with score > 60
 2789:     $join->add_database($scores, filter => { score => { '>' => 60 } });
 2790: 
 2791:     # Add a database whose join key has a different local name
 2792:     $join->add_database($stnames, join_column => 'state_code');
 2793: 
 2794:     # All three options combined, and chained

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
 2795:     $join->add_database($db4,
 2796:         join_column    => 'ref_id',
 2797:         filter         => { active => 1 },
 2798:         remove_columns => ['legacy_col'],
 2799:     );
 2800: 
 2801: =head3 PSEUDOCODE
 2802: 
 2803:     determine the new database's index (length of current _dbs array)
 2804:     extract the database object from positional or named argument
 2805:     croak if it is not a Database::Abstraction subclass
 2806:     register join_column alias in _join_map if different from canonical
 2807:     register filter in _filters if provided
 2808:     fetch column list from the new database

 2934: =head2 remove_column
 2935: 
 2936: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2937: 
 2938:     $join->remove_column('email');
 2939: 
 2940:     # Chainable
 2941:     $join->remove_column('internal_id')->remove_column('audit_ts');
 2942: 
 2943: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2944: 
 2945: Permanently hides a column from the merged view.  After this call:
 2946: 
 2947: =over 4
 2948: 
 2949: =item *
 2950: 
 2951: The column does not appear in C<columns()> or C<schema()>.
 2952: 
 2953: =item *
 2954: 
 2955: Returned row hashrefs do not contain the column key.
 2956: 
 2957: =item *
 2958: 
 2959: Any query criterion that references the removed column is silently dropped
 2960: (with a C<carp> warning).
 2961: 
 2962: =back
 2963: 
 2964: The C<join_column> cannot be removed; attempting to do so will C<croak>.
 2965: Removing a column that does not exist in any database is silently ignored
 2966: (the call is idempotent and safe).  The C<columns()> and C<schema()>

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 2967: memoisation caches are cleared automatically.
 2968: 
 2969: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2970: 
 2971: =head4 Input
 2972: 
 2973:     $col    Positional string: the column name to remove.
 2974: 
 2975:             DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any string; non-existent columns are silently
 2976:                                   ignored (idempotent call, returns $self).
 2977:             DOMAIN -- EP invalid: join_column value => croak error_remove_join_col.
 2978:             DOMAIN -- BVA:        undef and '' are explicit no-ops (returns $self).
 2979:                                   These are below the minimum meaningful string

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 2980:                                   length and are handled without any warning.
 2981: 
 2982: =head4 Output
 2983: 
 2984:     Returns C<$self> to support method chaining.
 2985: 
 2986: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2987: 
 2988:     # Hide private fields immediately after construction
 2989:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 2990:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
 2991:         join_column => 'entry',
 2992:     )->remove_column('email')
 2993:      ->remove_column('internal_notes');
 2994: 
 2995:     # Verify they are gone
 2996:     my $cols = $join->columns();
 2997:     # 'email' and 'internal_notes' are absent
 2998: 
 2999: =head3 MESSAGES
 3000: 
 3001:     error_remove_join_col -- attempt to remove the join_column itself
 3002: 
 3003: =cut
 3004: 
 3005: sub remove_column {
 3006: 	my ($self, $col) = @_;
 3007: 
 3008: 	# Premise: undef and '' are provably no-ops (nothing to remove).
 3009: 	# Conclusion: guard at the top eliminates two separate defined() checks below.
 3010: 	return $self unless defined $col && length $col;
 3011: 
 3012: 	# Premise: $col is defined (proven above) and join_col is always a non-empty string.
 3013: 	# Conclusion: direct string comparison is safe without a redundant defined() check.
 3014: 	croak $self->_err('error_remove_join_col', $col)
 3015: 		if $col eq $self->{_join_col};
 3016: 
 3017: 	$self->{_removed_cols}{$col} = 1;
 3018: 	delete $self->{_col_db}{$col};
 3019: 	$self->{_col_cache}    = undef;
 3020: 	$self->{_schema_cache} = undef;
 3021: 	$self->{_removed_list} = undef;	# invalidate the cached removed-column list
 3022: 
 3023: 	return $self;
 3024: }
 3025: 
 3026: =head2 query
 3027: 
 3028: Not supported.  C<Database::Join> does not implement the
 3029: C<Database::Abstraction::Query> chained builder because the builder's
 3030: C<.all()> / C<.first()> methods would be targeting a single component DA
 3031: rather than the merged view.  Calling this method will always C<croak>.
 3032: 
 3033: Use C<selectall_arrayref>, C<selectall_array>, C<fetchrow_hashref>,
 3034: C<count>, or C<each_row> against the C<Database::Join> object instead.
 3035: 
 3036: =cut

 3073: =head2 AUTOLOAD - column shortcut
 3074: 
 3075: Calling an unknown method whose name matches a visible column name performs
 3076: a column lookup across the merged view.
 3077: 
 3078: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 3079: 
 3080:     # Scalar context: value from the first matching row
 3081:     my $name  = $join->name(entry => 'C001');
 3082: 
 3083:     # List context: values from every matching row
 3084:     my @tiers = $join->tier();
 3085: 
 3086:     # With a positional join-key argument (when join_column is 'entry')
 3087:     my $score = $join->score('C001');
 3088: 
 3089: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 3090: 
 3091: AUTOLOAD routes the call to the appropriate component database by looking up
 3092: the column name in the internal column-ownership index.
 3093: 
 3094: When either C<join_map> or C<filters> is active, AUTOLOAD performs a full
 3095: join query instead of delegating directly to the owning database.  This is
 3096: necessary because:
 3097: 
 3098: =over 4
 3099: 
 3100: =item *
 3101: 
 3102: With C<join_map>, the owning database's primary key may differ from the
 3103: canonical join key used in the call arguments.
 3104: 
 3105: =item *
 3106: 
 3107: With C<filters>, bypassing the join would return rows that the filter is
 3108: meant to exclude.
 3109: 
 3110: =back
 3111: 
 3112: In list context, every matching merged row contributes one value to the
 3113: returned list.  In scalar context, only the first row's value is returned.
 3114: 
 3115: Calling a method whose name begins with C<_> (a private method) via AUTOLOAD
 3116: will C<croak> with a clear error message rather than being silently ignored.
 3117: 
 3118: =head3 EXAMPLE
 3119: 
 3120:     # Lookup a single customer's name (scalar context)
 3121:     my $name = $join->name('C001');   # 'C001' maps to entry => 'C001'
 3122:     print "Name: $name\n";
 3123: 
 3124:     # Get every tier value in the view (list context)
 3125:     my @all_tiers = $join->tier();
 3126:     my %freq;
 3127:     $freq{$_}++ for @all_tiers;
 3128: 
 3129:     # join_map active: AUTOLOAD runs a full join so the criteria are
 3130:     # translated correctly between the canonical and local key names.
 3131:     my @leesburg_states = sort $join->state('Leesburg');
 3132:     # ['Florida', 'Virginia'] if Leesburg appears in two states
 3133: 
 3134: =head3 PSEUDOCODE
 3135: 
 3136:     extract column name from $AUTOLOAD
 3137:     return if DESTROY
 3138:     croak if column name starts with '_' (private method guard)
 3139:     croak if column name is not in _col_db (unknown column)
 3140:     if join_map or filters are active:
 3141:         parse calling arguments using _parse_query_args

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
 3142:         call _joined_query to get all merged rows
 3143:         return map { $_->{col} } @rows  in list context
 3144:         return $rows[0]{col}            in scalar context
 3145:     else:
 3146:         delegate directly to the owning database
 3147: 
 3148: =head3 MESSAGES

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 3149:

 3216: # _parse_query_args( $self, $positional_key, @caller_args ) -> \%params
 3217: # Purpose: Normalise the three calling conventions used by every public query
 3218: #          method and AUTOLOAD into a single criteria hashref.
 3219: # Entry:   $positional_key -- the column name mapped to a bare scalar argument;
 3220: #          pass undef to use the join_column (the default for public methods).
 3221: # Exit:    Always returns a hashref; never undef.

 3223: 	my ($self, $key, @args) = @_;
 3224: 	# D~ elimination (Modus Tollens): when @args is empty the early return fires
 3225: 	# before $key is ever read, making the $key //= assignment a dead store.
 3226: 	# Moving the guard above the assignment removes the wasted hash dereference.
 3227: 	return {} unless @args;
 3228: 	$key //= $self->{_join_col};
 3229: 	return { $key => $args[0] }        if @args == 1 && !ref($args[0]);
 3230: 	return get_params(undef, @args) // {};
 3231: }

 3550: # _joined_query_array( \%params ) -> \@merged_rows
 3551: #
 3552: # Purpose: Core in-memory join algorithm.  Partitions criteria, fetches per-database
 3553: #          results, resolves the key set, and merges rows.
 3554: #
 3555: # Key-set resolution (applied for each secondary database after the primary):
 3556: #
 3557: #   If the database had criteria in this query call (after base filter overlay),
 3558: #   it acts as an INNER-JOIN partner: only keys present in its filtered result
 3559: #   survive.  This gives WHERE-clause semantics even under a LEFT join.
 3560: #
 3561: #   If the database had NO effective criteria:
 3562: #     inner  -> intersect  (standard inner join)
 3563: #     left   -> no change  (primary defines the key set)
 3564: #     outer  -> union      (all keys from any database)
 3565: #
 3566: # Row merge: for each qualifying primary row, secondary rows are overlaid in
 3567: # index order.  For duplicate columns, later databases win.  Local join-key
 3568: # aliases are renamed to the canonical join_column before merging.
 3569: # Removed columns are deleted from every merged row.
 3570: # _validate_pagination( $limit, $offset ) -> ($validated_limit, $validated_offset)

 3972: # _build_sqlite_cache()
 3973: # Purpose: Create (or rebuild) the persistent SQLite join cache.  Spills each
 3974: #          source database into a temp SQLite file using filter-only criteria;
 3975: #          SQLite-backed sources are zero-copy ATTACHed instead of spilled.
 3976: #          Query-time criteria are NOT applied here â€” they become WHERE clauses
 3977: #          in the per-call SQL generated by _sqlite_join.
 3978: # _sql_quote_identifier( $name ) -> $quoted
 3979: # Purpose: Produce a properly double-quoted SQL identifier, escaping any
 3980: #          embedded double-quote characters by doubling them (SQL standard).
 3981: #          Defence-in-depth: prevents SQL identifier injection when DA-supplied
 3982: #          column names or table names contain literal double-quote characters.
 3983: #          SQLite, like all ANSI SQL databases, represents a literal " inside a
 3984: #          double-quoted identifier as ""; this routine applies that transform.
 3985: # Entry:   $name â€” raw identifier string (column name, table name, or alias).
 3986: # Exit:    Returns the double-quoted, injection-safe SQL identifier string.
 3987: sub _sql_quote_identifier {
 3988: 	my ($name) = @_;
 3989: 	(my $safe = $name) =~ s/"/""/g;
 3990: 	return "\"$safe\"";
 3991: }

 5262: 		my $rv = $row->{$col};

 5264: 		if    ($op eq 'eq')       { return 0 unless $rv eq $val }
 5265: 		elsif ($op eq 'ne')       { return 0 unless $rv ne $val }

 5267: 		elsif ($op eq 'lte')      { return 0 unless $rv le $val }
 5268: 		elsif ($op eq 'gt')       { return 0 unless $rv gt $val }
 5269: 		elsif ($op eq 'gte')      { return 0 unless $rv ge $val }

 5273: 			my $re = join('', map {

 5276: 				: quotemeta($_)
 5277: 			} split //, $val);
 5278: 			return 0 unless $rv =~ /\A$re\z/s;
 5279: 		}
 5280: 	}

 5284: # _apply_distinct_array( \@rows, \@columns ) -> \@deduped_rows
 5285: # Purpose: Deduplicate an array of merged row hashrefs.
 5286: # Entry:   $rows is the full merged result arrayref (already sorted when called
 5287: #          from _joined_query_array â€” distinct is applied before sort, but
 5288: #          that is fine since dedup preserves relative order).

 5290: #          deduplicate on their combined value; when absent, deduplicate on all
 5291: #          columns (full-row equality).

 5314: # _merge_criteria( \%base, \%extra ) -> \%merged
 5315: # Purpose: Merge two criteria hashrefs for the same database column set.
 5316: # Entry:   %base is the permanent filter; %extra is the query-time criteria.
 5317: # Exit:    Returns a new hashref with both applied.
 5318: # Merging rule: when both values for the same column are operator hashrefs
 5319: #   (e.g. { '>' => 60 } and { '<' => 365 }), the operators are combined
 5320: #   so both constraints apply simultaneously (AND semantics).
 5321: #   Otherwise the extra (query-time) value overwrites the base value.
 5322: sub _merge_criteria :Protected {
●5323 → 5325 → 5334 5323: 	my ($base, $extra) = @_;
 5324: 	my %merged = %{$base};
 5325: 	for my $col (keys %{$extra}) {
 5326: 		if (exists $merged{$col}
 5327: 		        && ref($merged{$col}) eq 'HASH'
 5328: 		        && ref($extra->{$col}) eq 'HASH') {
 5329: 			$merged{$col} = { %{ $merged{$col} }, %{ $extra->{$col} } };
 5330: 		} else {
 5331: 			$merged{$col} = $extra->{$col};
 5332: 		}
 5333: 	}
 5334: 	return \%merged;
 5335: }

 5337: # _copy_criteria( \%criteria ) -> \%copy
 5338: # Purpose: Return a two-level deep copy of a single criteria hashref so that
 5339: #          post-construction mutation of the caller's hash cannot change the
 5340: #          stored filter.  Operator sub-hashrefs (e.g. { '>' => 80 }) are
 5341: #          shallow-copied one additional level, matching the broadcast-copy
 5342: #          idiom used in _partition_criteria for join-column criteria.
 5343: # Entry:   $criteria is a hashref (may be undef).
 5344: # Exit:    Returns a new hashref; never returns the input reference itself.
 5345: sub _copy_criteria {
 5346: 	my ($criteria) = @_;
 5347: 	return {} unless $criteria && %{$criteria};
 5348: 	return {
 5349: 		map {
 5350: 			$_ => ref($criteria->{$_}) eq 'HASH'
 5351: 				? { %{ $criteria->{$_} } }  # shallow-copy operator sub-hashref
 5352: 				: $criteria->{$_}
 5353: 		} keys %{$criteria}
 5354: 	};
 5355: }
 5356: 
 5357: # _copy_filters( \%filters ) -> \%copy
 5358: # Purpose: Deep-copy the filters hashref (db_index => criteria_hashref) so
 5359: #          that post-construction mutation of the caller's hash cannot silently
 5360: #          bypass the inner-join row-security guarantee.
 5361: # Entry:   $filters may be undef.
 5362: # Exit:    Returns a new hashref; never returns the input reference itself.
 5363: sub _copy_filters {
 5364: 	my ($filters) = @_;
 5365: 	return {} unless $filters && %{$filters};
 5366: 	return { map { $_ => _copy_criteria($filters->{$_}) } keys %{$filters} };
 5367: }
