 5390: 
 5391: =head1 ENCODING
 5392: 
 5393: C<Database::Join> is a routing and merge layer written in pure Perl.  It never
 5394: inspects, re-encodes, or transforms the I<content> of strings -- only their
 5395: identity (via C<eq> / hash-key equality) and, on the array backend, their
 5396: numeric interpretation (via C<looks_like_number>).
 5397: 
 5398: =over 4
 5399: 
 5400: =item Column names
 5401: 
 5402: Column names are exactly what each component C<Database::Abstraction> object
 5403: returns from its own C<columns()> method.  C<Database::Join> compares them with
 5404: C<eq> (byte-level equality) and builds its internal routing index from them.
 5405: ASCII column names (letters, digits, underscore) are safe everywhere.
 5406: Non-ASCII column names work as long as every component database returns them
 5407: in the same byte encoding and your Perl source file is loaded in the same
 5408: encoding.  Mixed encodings between component databases are not supported.
 5409: 
 5410: =item Join-key values (the join_column)
 5411: 
 5412: The join-column value is used as a hash key internally (C<$hash{$key}>).
 5413: Perl uses byte-level equality for hash keys, so any value that round-trips
 5414: correctly through your component databases works as a join key, including
 5415: non-ASCII strings, emoji, and other Unicode text, as long as all component
 5416: databases return the same bytes for the same conceptual key.
 5417: 
 5418:     # A Japanese city name as a join key works fine
 5419:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(entry => "\x{6771}\x{4EAC}");
 5420: 
 5421: =item Criteria values and row data
 5422: 
 5423: Criterion values and merged row values are passed verbatim between the caller
 5424: and the component databases.  C<Database::Join> does not examine them.  Full
 5425: UTF-8 text (including multi-byte characters, accented letters, CJK ideographs,
 5426: and emoji) round-trips safely provided the underlying component databases handle
 5427: UTF-8 correctly.
 5428: 
 5429:     # Emoji in a criterion value -- works if the DA supports it
 5430:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(mood => "\x{1F600}");
 5431: 
 5432: =item SQLite backend: UTF-8 round-trip guarantee
 5433: 
 5434: When the SQLite path is active (C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'> or C<'auto'>),
 5435: values are inserted into the temporary C<DBD::SQLite> database exclusively
 5436: through DBI prepared-statement placeholders -- never by string interpolation.
 5437: C<DBD::SQLite> operates in UTF-8 mode by default (C<sqlite_unicode =E<gt> 1>
 5438: in newer releases), so multi-byte UTF-8 text round-trips without corruption.
 5439: Binary blobs containing null bytes may not round-trip correctly; use the array
 5440: backend if you need binary-safe joins.
 5441: 
 5442: =item Sorting on the array backend uses Perl string comparison
 5443: 
 5444: The array backend sorts with Perl's C<cmp> (byte-level lexicographic order).
 5445: This is correct for pure-ASCII data and for consistently-encoded UTF-8 data
 5446: where byte order matches Unicode code-point order (e.g. sorting within a
 5447: single script).  For locale-aware sorting (e.g. sorting German umlauts in
 5448: German alphabetical order) switch to the SQLite backend, which delegates to
 5449: SQLite's C<ORDER BY> (locale support depends on how SQLite was compiled).
 5450: 
 5451: =item Measure aliases in aggregate()
 5452: 
 5453: The C<as> alias for each measure must match C</^\w+\z/a> -- ASCII word
 5454: characters only (letters, digits, underscore).  Full Unicode identifiers
 5455: are not supported as aliases.
 5456: 
 5457: =item i18n messages
 5458: 
 5459: All internal error and warning messages route through the optional C<i18n>
 5460: object via a C<translate($key, @args)> call.  The translation dictionary
 5461: controls the final encoding and character set of those user-visible strings.
 5462: 
 5463: =back
 5464: 
 5465: =head1 MESSAGES
 5466: 
 5467: The following messages can be produced by C<Database::Join>.  All messages
 5468: can be localised by supplying an C<i18n> object to C<new>.
 5469: 
 5470: =over 4
 5471: 
 5472: =item C<error_no_databases>
 5473: 
 5474: B<When:> The C<databases> arrayref passed to C<new> is empty.
 5475: 
 5476: B<Fix:> Pass at least one C<Database::Abstraction> subclass object.
 5477: 
 5478: =item C<error_invalid_db>
 5479: 
 5480: B<When:> An element of the C<databases> array (or the argument to
 5481: C<add_database>) is not an object, or is not a C<Database::Abstraction>
 5482: subclass.
 5483: 
 5484: B<Fix:> Instantiate the component database with its own C<new> method before
 5485: passing it to C<Database::Join>.
 5486: 
 5487: =item C<error_join_col_missing>
 5488: 
 5489: B<When:> The join key column (or its C<join_map> alias) does not exist in one
 5490: of the component databases.
 5491: 
 5492: B<Fix:> Either add the column to the database, change C<join_column> to a
 5493: column that is present everywhere, or use C<join_map> to declare the local
 5494: alias for databases that call it something different.
 5495: 
 5496: =item C<error_remove_join_col>
 5497: 
 5498: B<When:> C<remove_column> is called with the name of the join key column.
 5499: 
 5500: B<Fix:> The join key is required for the merge to work and cannot be hidden.
 5501: Remove a different column.
 5502: 
 5503: =item C<error_invalid_prefix>
 5504: 
 5505: B<When:> A value in the C<collision_prefix> hashref is a reference (e.g. a
 5506: hashref or arrayref) rather than a plain string.
 5507: 
 5508: B<Fix:> All C<collision_prefix> values must be plain strings.  A reference
 5509: would be stringified to C<HASH(0x...)> or C<ARRAY(0x...)>, leaking a heap
 5510: address into every column name returned by C<columns()>, C<schema()>, and all
 5511: query results.  Pass a plain string such as C<'db2'> or C<'secondary'>.
 5512: 
 5513: =item C<warn_unknown_column> (carp)
 5514: 
 5515: B<When:> A criterion is passed for a column that does not exist in any
 5516: component database (or has been removed with C<remove_column>).
 5517: 
 5518: B<Fix:> Check the column name spelling.  The criterion is ignored.
 5519: 
 5520: =item C<error_join_criterion>
 5521: 
 5522: B<When:> A criterion hashref contains a C<join =E<gt>> key (the
 5523: C<Database::Abstraction> SQL-JOIN syntax).
 5524: 
 5525: B<Fix:> C<join =E<gt>> targets a single table inside one DA; it cannot be
 5526: routed through a merged view.  Express multi-table relationships by adding
 5527: the joined table as a separate C<Database::Abstraction> object in the
 5528: C<databases =E<gt> []> constructor list instead.
 5529: 
 5530: =item C<error_query_unsupported>
 5531: 
 5532: B<When:> C<query()> is called on a C<Database::Join> object.
 5533: 
 5534: B<Fix:> Use C<selectall_arrayref>, C<selectall_array>, C<fetchrow_hashref>,
 5535: or C<count> instead.
 5536: 
 5537: =item C<error_group_by_unsupported>
 5538: 
 5539: B<When:> C<group_by()> is called on a C<Database::Join> object.
 5540: 
 5541: B<Fix:> Use C<aggregate()> instead.  The fluent C<Database::Abstraction::Query>
 5542: chain targets a single component DA, not the merged view.
 5543: 
 5544: =item C<error_execute_unsupported>
 5545: 
 5546: B<When:> C<execute()> is called on a C<Database::Join> object.
 5547: 
 5548: B<Fix:> Use the Perl-level query methods instead.  Raw SQL cannot span
 5549: heterogeneous database backends.
 5550: 
 5551: =item C<error_invalid_backend>
 5552: 
 5553: B<When:> The C<backend> parameter passed to C<new> is not one of C<'array'>,
 5554: C<'sqlite'>, or C<'auto'>.
 5555: 
 5556: B<Fix:> Use exactly one of those three strings.  The check is case-sensitive;
 5557: C<'SQLite'> or C<'Auto'> will not be accepted.
 5558: 
 5559: =item C<error_sqlite_connect>
 5560: 
 5561: B<When:> The SQLite join backend fails to open the temporary SQLite database
 5562: file.  Common causes: the C<tmpdir> directory is not writable, the filesystem
 5563: has no free space, or C<DBD::SQLite> is not installed.
 5564: 
 5565: B<Fix:> Check that the directory given by C<tmpdir> (or the system temp
 5566: directory if C<tmpdir> was not set) is writable and has sufficient free space.
 5567: Verify that C<DBD::SQLite> 1.70 or later is installed.
 5568: 
 5569: =item C<error_aggregate_measures_required>
 5570: 
 5571: B<When:> C<aggregate()> is called without a C<measures> key, or with
 5572: C<measures => []>.
 5573: 
 5574: B<Fix:> Pass a non-empty arrayref: C<< measures => [{ fn => 'count', as => 'n' }] >>.
 5575: 
 5576: =item C<error_aggregate_measure_ref>
 5577: 
 5578: B<When:> One of the elements in the C<measures> arrayref is not a hashref (it
 5579: is a scalar, arrayref, or other non-hash reference).
 5580: 
 5581: B<Fix:> Every element of C<measures> must be a plain hashref with at least
 5582: C<fn> and C<as> keys.
 5583: 
 5584: =item C<error_aggregate_fn_unknown>
 5585: 
 5586: B<When:> The C<fn> value in a measure hashref is not one of: C<sum>, C<avg>,
 5587: C<min>, C<max>, C<count>, C<count_distinct> (case-insensitive).
 5588: 
 5589: B<Fix:> Use a supported function name.  C<median>, C<stddev> and window
 5590: functions are not implemented.
 5591: 
 5592: =item C<error_aggregate_col_required>
 5593: 
 5594: B<When:> The C<col> field is missing from a measure whose C<fn> requires a
 5595: column (any function except plain C<count>).
 5596: 
 5597: B<Fix:> Add C<< col => 'column_name' >> to the measure hashref.  C<count>
 5598: without C<col> emits C<COUNT(*)>; all other functions require a column.
 5599: 
 5600: =item C<error_aggregate_col_unknown>
 5601: 
 5602: B<When:> The C<col> in a measure, or a name in C<by>, refers to a column that
 5603: is not in the merged view (either never existed or was hidden with
 5604: C<remove_column>).
 5605: 
 5606: B<Fix:> Check C<< $join->columns() >> for the correct published name.
 5607: C<collision_prefix>-renamed columns are accessed as C<"prefix.col">.
 5608: 
 5609: =item C<error_aggregate_as_required>
 5610: 
 5611: B<When:> A measure hashref has no C<as> key, or its value is C<undef> or the
 5612: empty string.
 5613: 
 5614: B<Fix:> Every measure must have a non-empty C<as> alias: C<< as => 'total' >>.
 5615: 
 5616: =item C<error_aggregate_as_invalid>
 5617: 
 5618: B<When:> A measure alias (C<as>) contains characters other than word
 5619: characters (C<\w>: letters, digits, underscore).
 5620: 
 5621: B<Fix:> Use a plain word for the alias: C<< as => 'total' >>, not
 5622: C<< as => 'total score' >> or C<< as => 'total-score' >>.
 5623: 
 5624: =item C<error_aggregate_as_duplicate>
 5625: 
 5626: B<When:> Two or more measures share the same C<as> alias.
 5627: 
 5628: B<Fix:> Choose a unique alias for each measure.
 5629: 
 5630: =item C<error_aggregate_as_collision>
 5631: 
 5632: B<When:> A measure's C<as> alias is the same as one of the C<by> column names.
 5633: 
 5634: B<Fix:> Choose an alias that does not match any grouping dimension.
 5635: 
 5636: =item C<error_aggregate_by_invalid>
 5637: 
 5638: B<When:> The C<by> parameter is present but is not an arrayref.
 5639: 
 5640: B<Fix:> Pass an arrayref (possibly empty for a grand total):
 5641: C<< by => ['region'] >> or C<< by => [] >>.
 5642: 
 5643: =item C<error_aggregate_having_key>
 5644: 
 5645: B<When:> A key in the C<having> hashref is not one of the C<by> column names,
 5646: a measure alias, C<-or>, or C<-and>.
 5647: 
 5648: B<Fix:> Only filter on names that appear in the aggregate output.
 5649: 
 5650: =item C<error_aggregate_having_operator>
 5651: 
 5652: B<When:> An operator hashref inside C<having> uses an operator that is not in
 5653: C<%SAFE_SQL_OPS>, C<%SAFE_LIST_OPS>, or C<%SAFE_NOARG_OPS>.
 5654: 
 5655: B<Fix:> Use a supported operator.  C<BETWEEN> is not supported; write it as
 5656: two constraints: C<< { '>=' => $lo, '<=' => $hi } >>.
 5657: 
 5658: =item C<warn_aggregate_sort_key> (carp)
 5659: 
 5660: B<When:> The column or alias named by C<sort_by> in C<aggregate()> is not one
 5661: of the C<by> columns or measure aliases.
 5662: 
 5663: B<Fix:> Use a column name from C<by> or a measure alias from C<measures>.
 5664: The sort falls back to the default group ordering (C<by> columns ascending).
 5665: 
 5666: =item C<warn_aggregate_non_numeric> (carp)
 5667: 
 5668: B<When:> The array backend encounters one or more non-numeric values in a
 5669: column that is the target of a C<sum> or C<avg> measure.  The SQLite backend
 5670: coerces non-numeric values to C<0.0> via C<CAST> and emits no warning.
 5671: 
 5672: B<Fix:> Clean the data before joining, or switch to the SQLite backend if
 5673: coercion to zero is acceptable.
 5674: 
 5675:     # Example: the warning names the column, the count, and the first bad value
 5676:     # "aggregate: column "score" has 2 non-numeric value(s), skipped, e.g. "n/a""
 5677: 
 5678: =back

 5700: =head1 SECURITY CONSIDERATIONS
 5701: 
 5702: C<Database::Join> is a pure in-memory routing and merge layer.  It never
 5703: generates SQL strings, never opens files, and never calls C<system()>,
 5704: C<exec()>, or C<eval()>.  The security properties described below are
 5705: architectural guarantees, not run-time checks.
 5706: 
 5707: =head2 What Database::Join guarantees
 5708: 
 5709: =over 4
 5710: 
 5711: =item Criteria partition isolation
 5712: 
 5713: Every criterion you pass to a query method is routed to I<exactly one>
 5714: component database (the one that owns that column), or to I<all> databases
 5715: when the criterion is on the join key column.  A hostile value in a criterion
 5716: for column C<name> (owned by database A) will never reach database B.
 5717: 
 5718: =item Unknown columns are rejected before reaching any database
 5719: 
 5720: If a criterion column name is not present in any component database (or has
 5721: been hidden with C<remove_column>), C<Database::Join> logs a C<carp> warning
 5722: and silently drops the criterion.  No database receives the hostile key.
 5723: 
 5724: =item AUTOLOAD only accepts word-character column names
 5725: 
 5726: Perl's method dispatch extracts the column name via C<\w+>, which matches only
 5727: C<[A-Za-z0-9_]>.  Hostile method names with shell metacharacters, quotes, or
 5728: spaces cannot reach the AUTOLOAD dispatch path.  Private names (starting with
 5729: C<_>) are additionally blocked with an explicit C<croak>.
 5730: 
 5731: =item No value sanitisation (by design)
 5732: 
 5733: C<Database::Join> does I<not> sanitise, HTML-encode, or validate the
 5734: I<values> in criteria hashrefs.  Preventing SQL injection is the
 5735: responsibility of the underlying C<Database::Abstraction> objects (which use
 5736: parameterised queries).  Preventing XSS or header injection is the
 5737: responsibility of the CGI or web layer that renders the output.
 5738: 
 5739: =item Taint-mode compatible (array path)
 5740: 
 5741: The array merge path contains no C<system()>, C<exec()>, backtick,
 5742: C<open(PIPE)>, or C<eval STRING> calls.  It neither opens files nor constructs
 5743: shell commands.  The AUTOLOAD regex C</ :: (\w++) \z /x> uses a possessive
 5744: quantifier (C<\w++>) and a strict end-of-string anchor (C<\z>) and produces
 5745: an I<untainted> capture, so the column name used for dispatch is clean under
 5746: C<-T>.  Criteria values are passed verbatim to component C<Database::Abstraction>
 5747: objects; those objects are responsible for handling tainted values at the SQL
 5748: parameterisation layer.
 5749: 
 5750: =item SQLite backend: column names are quoted, values are parametrised
 5751: 
 5752: When the SQLite path is active, C<Database::Join> generates SQL internally.
 5753: All column and table names are double-quoted (SQL identifier quoting) before
 5754: being embedded in statement strings.  All row values are passed to SQLite
 5755: exclusively through DBI prepared statement placeholders -- never by string
 5756: interpolation.  A hostile value in a source row therefore cannot inject SQL
 5757: into the temporary database.
 5758: 
 5759: The temporary SQLite file is created by C<File::Temp> using a securely random,
 5760: unpredictable filename.  No C<system()> or shell command is used to create or
 5761: remove it.  The connection is made with C<DBI-E<gt>connect(..., { RaiseError =E<gt> 1,
 5762: PrintError =E<gt> 0 })> and is closed before the method returns.  Column names
 5763: in the generated SQL come from C<columns()>, which is produced by
 5764: C<Database::Abstraction> at construction time -- they are not derived from
 5765: caller-supplied criteria values.
 5766: 
 5767: =item Operator hashref broadcast copy
 5768: 
 5769: When the same join-key criterion (an operator hashref such as
 5770: C<< { '>' => 'A' } >>) is broadcast to multiple component databases, each
 5771: database receives its own I<shallow copy> of the hashref.  A component database
 5772: that mutates the hashref's contents at the top level cannot affect what
 5773: subsequent databases receive.
 5774: 
 5775: =item collision_prefix value type guard
 5776: 
 5777: C<_build_col_index> rejects any C<collision_prefix> value that is a reference
 5778: (hashref, arrayref, coderef, etc.) with an immediate C<croak>.  A reference
 5779: value would stringify to C<"HASH(0x...)">, leaking a heap address into every
 5780: column name, C<columns()> listing, and merged row returned to the caller.  The
 5781: guard fires before any column name is constructed.
 5782: 
 5783: =item Filter deep-copy isolation
 5784: 
 5785: The C<filters> constructor parameter and the C<filter> option of
 5786: C<add_database()> are I<deep-copied> at the point of use.  The caller's
 5787: original hashrefs are never stored; post-construction mutation of those
 5788: hashrefs cannot widen or bypass the configured row-security constraints.
 5789: 
 5790: =item mandatory_filters cannot be overridden or bypassed at query time
 5791: 
 5792: Predicates stored in C<_mandatory_filters> are applied unconditionally to every
 5793: query result, after the in-memory merge (array path) or as parameterised SQL
 5794: C<WHERE> clauses (SQLite path).  There is no query-time parameter that suppresses
 5795: or modifies them.  Caller-supplied criteria are ANDed with the mandatory
 5796: predicates -- they can only further restrict the result set, never widen it.
 5797: 
 5798: Each predicate is a shallow copy of the caller-supplied hashref; post-construction
 5799: mutation of the original C<mandatory_filters> arrayref or its elements has no
 5800: effect on the stored predicates.
 5801: 
 5802: Values are always passed to the SQLite engine via DBI bind parameters, never by
 5803: string interpolation.  A hostile C<val> in a predicate cannot inject SQL.
 5804: 
 5805: =back
 5806: 
 5807: =head2 What the caller is responsible for
 5808: 
 5809: =over 4
 5810: 
 5811: =item Sanitise values before building criteria
 5812: 
 5813: DJ passes criterion values verbatim to component databases.  If your
 5814: application accepts user-supplied filter values (e.g. from a CGI query
 5815: string), those values I<must> be validated or sanitised by your application
 5816: before being passed to DJ.
 5817: 
 5818: =item Restrict which columns the caller can filter on
 5819: 
 5820: Any column in C<columns()> can be used as a filter criterion.  If a column
 5821: should not be filterable by end users (e.g. an internal status flag), hide it
 5822: with C<remove_column> so that queries on it are silently dropped.
 5823: 
 5824: =item Do not expose the joined view directly to user-supplied criteria
 5825: 
 5826: DJ is not a firewall.  It faithfully routes user input to component databases.
 5827: Wrap DJ calls in a thin service layer that whitelists the permitted criterion
 5828: columns and validates their values.
 5829: 
 5830: =back
 5831: 
 5832: =head3 API SPECIFICATION (security surface)
 5833: 
 5834:     Input accepted by all query methods and passed through DJ to component databases:
 5835: 
 5836:     Criterion values:
 5837:         type: scalar string | operator hashref { OP => scalar }
 5838:         validation: NONE (DJ trusts the caller; component DA is responsible)
 5839:         max size: unconstrained (OOM risk on very large values)
 5840: 
 5841:     Column name keys in criteria:
 5842:         type: string
 5843:         validation: must be present in _col_db (else carp + drop)
 5844:         character set: any Perl string (including control chars); DJ does
 5845:                        not impose a character-set restriction on criteria KEYS
 5846: 
 5847:     AUTOLOAD method-name-as-column:
 5848:         type: \w+ (enforced by Perl regex /::(\w+)$/)
 5849:         validation: must not start with '_'; must be in _col_db
 5850: 
 5851: =encoding UTF-8
 5852: 
 5853: =head1 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 5854: 
 5855: Z calculus schemas for the key invariants and operations.
 5856: Unicode is used throughout this section as required by Z notation.

 5989: =head2 SECURITY INVARIANTS
 5990: 
 5991:     â”€â”€â”€ PartitionIsolation â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€

 5993:     âˆ€ i : 0 â€¥ #dbs-1 â€¢

 5995:

 5998:         (âˆ€ i : 0 â€¥ #dbs-1 â€¢ col âˆ‰ dom(per_db(i)))

 6000:     â”€â”€â”€ NoCodeExecution â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 6001:     -- DJ contains no call to system(), exec(), open(PIPE), or eval().
 6002:     -- Hostile criterion values therefore cannot achieve code execution
 6003:     -- within the Database::Join layer.
 6004:     âˆ€ v : VALUE â€¢ _joined_query({col â†¦ v}) â‰  âŠ¥ due to code injection

 6006: =head2 filters
 6007: 
 6008:     â”€â”€â”€ Filters â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 6009:     filters  : â„• â‡¸ CRITERIA
 6010:     dbs      : seq DATABASE_ABSTRACTION
 6011:     â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 6012:     dom filters âŠ† 0 â€¥ (#dbs - 1)
 6013: 
 6014:     -- A filtered database i always contributes to key-set intersection.
 6015:     -- For each query with criteria C:
 6016:     effective_criteria(i, C) ==
 6017:         if i âˆˆ dom filters
 6018:         then merge_criteria(filters(i), partition(C, i))
 6019:         else partition(C, i)
 6020: 
 6021:     -- Criteria merging (AND semantics for operator hashrefs):
 6022:     merge_criteria(base, extra) ==
 6023:         { col : dom base âˆª dom extra â€¢
 6024:             if col âˆˆ dom base âˆ© dom extra
 6025:                âˆ§ base(col) âˆˆ HASHREF âˆ§ extra(col) âˆˆ HASHREF
 6026:             then col â†¦ base(col) âˆª extra(col)   -- operator union
 6027:             else col â†¦ (if col âˆˆ dom extra then extra(col) else base(col)) }
 6028: 
 6029: =head2 base_criteria
 6030: 
 6031:     â”€â”€â”€ BaseCriteria â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 6032:     base_criteria : CRITERIA              -- column-name keyed
 6033:     col_db        : NAME â‡¸ â„•
 6034:     filters       : â„• â‡¸ CRITERIA         -- after Init
 6035:     â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 6036:     -- base_criteria is applied once at construction by partitioning
 6037:     -- its columns into per-db slices and merging into filters:
 6038:     âˆ€ i : 0 â€¥ #dbs-1 â€¢
 6039:         bc_slice(i) = partition(base_criteria, i)
 6040:         filters(i)  = merge_criteria(bc_slice(i), filters_explicit(i))
 6041: 
 6042:     -- where filters_explicit is the filters parameter as supplied.
 6043:     -- merge_criteria semantics: explicit filters win on plain-scalar
 6044:     -- conflicts; operator hashrefs are combined (AND).
 6045: 
 6046:     -- Unknown columns are dropped (warn_unknown_column carp);
 6047:     -- the join_column is broadcast to all databases.
 6048: 
 6049:     -- Key-set semantics: any database that receives a non-empty
 6050:     -- bc_slice acts as an inner-join partner (same as filters).
 6051: 
 6052: =head2 aggregate
 6053: 
 6054:     â”€â”€â”€ AggregateSpec â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 6055:     MEASURE ::= { fn : AGGFN; col : NAME?; as : ALIAS }
 6056:     AGGFN   ::= sum | avg | min | max | count | count_distinct
 6057:     ALIAS   ::= \w+
 6058:     SPEC    ::= { by : seq NAME; measures : seq MEASURE;
 6059:                   having : CRITERIA?;
 6060:                   sort_by : NAME Ã— {ASC,DESC}?; limit : â„•?; offset : â„•? }
 6061:     â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 6062:     -- Pre-conditions (checked by _validate_aggregate_spec):
 6063:     âˆ€ m : measures â€¢
 6064:         m.fn âˆˆ {sum, avg, min, max, count, count_distinct}
 6065:         âˆ§ (m.fn â‰  count âˆ¨ m.fn = count_distinct âŸ¹ m.col â‰  âˆ…)
 6066:         âˆ§ m.as matches /^\w+\z/
 6067:     âˆ€ name : by â€¢ name âˆˆ dom col_db
 6068:     âˆ€ m : measures â€¢ m.col â‰  âˆ… âŸ¹ m.col âˆˆ dom col_db
 6069:     # aliases are unique and do not collide with 'by' names:
 6070:     âˆ€ i,j : 0 â€¥ #measures-1 â€¢ i â‰  j âŸ¹ measures(i).as â‰  measures(j).as
 6071:     âˆ€ m : measures; b : by â€¢ m.as â‰  b
 6072: 
 6073:     â”€â”€â”€ Aggregate â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 6074:     ÎžDatabase_Join               -- state unchanged (Xi-transition)
 6075:     criteria?  : CRITERIA        -- pre-aggregation WHERE
 6076:     spec?      : SPEC
 6077:     result!    : seq GROUP_ROW
 6078:     â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 6079:     -- GROUP_ROW has exactly the by-columns plus the measure aliases.
 6080:     -- Group key: tuple of by-column values from a merged row.
 6081:     group_key(row, by) == { b : by â€¢ b â†¦ row(b) }
 6082: 
 6083:     -- Fold function on the array backend:
 6084:     fold(rows, spec) ==
 6085:         let G = { k : IMAGE(group_key(r, by) | r : rows) â€¢ k â†¦ {} }
 6086:         for r : rows:
 6087:             G[group_key(r, by)] += r   -- accumulate per measure.fn
 6088:         for g : dom G:
 6089:             G[g] = { b : by â€¢ b â†¦ g(b) }
 6090:                  âˆª { m : measures â€¢ m.as â†¦ apply(m.fn, m.col, G[g].rows) }
 6091:         result_pre = ran G
 6092: 
 6093:     -- apply: maps aggregate function to its computed value.
 6094:     apply(count, âˆ…, rows)         == #rows
 6095:     apply(count_distinct, col, rows) == #{ r : rows â€¢ r(col) }
 6096:     apply(sum, col, rows)         == Î£ { r : rows | is_num(r(col)) â€¢ r(col) }
 6097:     apply(avg, col, rows)         == sum(col, rows) / #{ r | is_num(r(col)) }
 6098:     apply(min, col, rows)         == min { r : rows â€¢ r(col) }
 6099:     apply(max, col, rows)         == max { r : rows â€¢ r(col) }
 6100: 
 6101:     -- Post-aggregation HAVING filter:
 6102:     result_having = [ g : result_pre | matches_having(g, spec.having) ]
 6103: 
 6104:     -- Grand total (by = []):
 6105:     #by = 0 âŸ¹ #result! = 1
 6106:     #by > 0 âˆ§ #result_having = 0 âŸ¹ result! = []
 6107: 
 6108:     -- Final result:
 6109:     result! = sort_and_page(result_having, spec)
 6110: 
 6111:     -- Backend dispatch:
 6112:     backend âˆˆ {sqlite, auto} âŸ¹ aggregation pushed to SQLite GROUP BY
 6113:     backend = array           âŸ¹ fold() applied in Perl
 6114: 
 6115:     -- Correctness invariant (both paths return identical rows):
 6116:     âˆ€ criteria?, spec? â€¢
 6117:         _sqlite_aggregate(criteria?, spec?) = _fold_aggregate(rows(criteria?), spec?)
 6118: 
 6119: =head2 selectall_arrayref
 6120: 
 6121:     selectall_arrayref : CRITERIA â†’ seq MERGED_ROW
 6122:     pre:  âˆ€ col : dom criteria â€¢ col âˆˆ dom self._col_db âˆª {self._join_col}
 6123:     post: result = _joined_query(criteria)
 6124:           result is sorted ascending by join_col value
 6125: 
 6126: =head2 selectall_array
 6127: 
 6128:     selectall_array : CRITERIA â†’ seq MERGED_ROW | MERGED_ROW?
 6129:     pre:  same as selectall_arrayref
 6130:     post: wantarray  => result = @{ selectall_arrayref(criteria) }
 6131:           !wantarray => result = selectall_arrayref(criteria)[0]  (or undef)
 6132: 
 6133: =head2 fetchrow_hashref
 6134: 
 6135:     fetchrow_hashref : CRITERIA â†’ MERGED_ROW?
 6136:     post: result = selectall_arrayref(criteria)[0]  (or undef if empty)
 6137: 
 6138: =head2 count
 6139: 
 6140:     count : CRITERIA â†’ â„•
 6141:     post: result = #selectall_arrayref(criteria)
 6142: 
 6143: =head2 columns
 6144: 
 6145:     columns : â†’ seq NAME
 6146:     post: result = sort(
 6147:               (â‹ƒ { i : 0 â€¥ #dbs-1 â€¢ ran(dbs(i).columns) }
 6148:                \ dom removed_cols
 6149:                \ { local_jc(i) | i âˆˆ dom join_map âˆ§ local_jc(i) â‰  join_col })
 6150:           )
 6151: 
 6152: =head2 schema
 6153: 
 6154:     schema : â†’ NAME â‡¸ SCHEMA_INFO
 6155:     post: dom(result) = ran(columns())
 6156:           âˆ€ col : dom(result) â€¢
 6157:               result(col) = (last database containing col).schema()(col)
 6158: 
 6159: =head2 updated
 6160: 
 6161:     updated : â†’ â„•
 6162:     post: result = max { i : 0 â€¥ #dbs-1 â€¢ dbs(i).updated() }
 6163: 
 6164: =head2 remove_column
 6165: 
 6166:     remove_column : NAME â†’ Database_Join
 6167:     pre:  col â‰  self._join_col
 6168:     post: self'._removed_cols = self._removed_cols âˆª {col}
 6169:           self'._col_db       = self._col_db \ {col}
 6170:           self'._col_cache    = undef
 6171:           self'._schema_cache = undef
 6172: 
 6173: =head2 AUTOLOAD
 6174: 
 6175:     AUTOLOAD : NAME Ã— CRITERIA â†’ VALUE | seq VALUE
 6176:     pre:  col âˆˆ dom self._col_db
 6177:           col does not begin with '_'
 6178:     post: let rows = _joined_query(criteria)
 6179:           wantarray  => result = { r : rows â€¢ r(col) }
 6180:           !wantarray => result = rows(0)(col)  (or undef if rows is empty)
 6181: 
 6182: =head2 backend
 6183: 
 6184:     â”€â”€â”€ BackendDispatch â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 6185:     backend        : {array, sqlite, auto}
 6186:     max_array_rows : â„•
 6187:     â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 6188: 
 6189:     -- row_count(db): cheaply count rows in a component database.
 6190:     -- Uses dbi_source() COUNT(*) SQL for SQLite-backed sources,
 6191:     -- or the DA's own count() method when defined in its own package.
 6192:     -- Returns âŠ¥ (bottom / unknown) when neither is available.
 6193:     row_count(db) ==
 6194:         if (db has dbi_source() returning a SQLite dbh)
 6195:         then SELECT COUNT(*) FROM source_table
 6196:         else if (defined &{ref(db) ^ "::count"})
 6197:         then db.count()
 6198:         else âŠ¥
 6199: 
 6200:     -- Combined row count across all component databases.
 6201:     -- If any database returns âŠ¥, total is âŠ¥ (cannot determine).
 6202:     total_count ==
 6203:         if âˆ€ i : 0 â€¥ #dbs-1 â€¢ row_count(dbs i) â‰  âŠ¥
 6204:         then Î£ { i : 0 â€¥ #dbs-1 â€¢ row_count(dbs i) }
 6205:         else âŠ¥
 6206: 
 6207:     -- Dispatch rule for _joined_query:
 6208:     use_sqlite(C) ==
 6209:         backend = 'sqlite'
 6210:         âˆ¨ (backend = 'auto' âˆ§ total_count â‰  âŠ¥ âˆ§ total_count > max_array_rows)
 6211: 
 6212:     _joined_query(C) ==
 6213:         if use_sqlite(C)
 6214:         then _sqlite_join(C)
 6215:         else _joined_query_array(C)
 6216: 
 6217:     -- Result identity invariant: both paths return identical rows.
 6218:     âˆ€ C : CRITERIA â€¢
 6219:         _sqlite_join(C) = _joined_query_array(C)
 6220: 
 6221: =head1 STATE DIAGRAM
 6222: 
 6223: C<Database::Join> objects follow three independent finite state machines (FSMs).
 6224: Each FSM is described with an ASCII diagram showing valid states (boxes), the
 6225: triggers that cause transitions (arrows), and important side-effects.
 6226: 
 6227: =head2 FSM 1: Object Lifecycle
 6228: 
 6229: Governs the structural state of a C<Database::Join> instance.
 6230: Query methods (C<selectall_arrayref>, C<selectall_array>, C<fetchrow_hashref>,
 6231: C<count>, C<aggregate>, C<each_row>, C<columns>, C<schema>, C<updated>) are
 6232: schema-preserving (Xi-transitions) and are not shown because they do not change
 6233: state.  C<aggregate()> additionally triggers the SQLite cache lifecycle (FSM 2)
 6234: when the backend is C<'sqlite'> or C<'auto'>, just like any other query method.
 6235: 
 6236:     [pre-creation]
 6237:          |
 6238:          | new( databases => [...], join_column => '...' )
 6239:          |   Side-effect: _col_db routing table built;
 6240:          |                _autoload_pk set from autoload_pk param (default: join_column)
 6241:          v
 6242:     [CONSTRUCTED] <-----------------------------------------+
 6243:          |    |                                              |
 6244:          |    +--------------------------------------------+ |
 6245:          |    (query methods: no structural change)         | |
 6246:          |                                                  | |
 6247:          |-- remove_column( col ) -------> [COL_REMOVED] <--+ |
 6248:          |                                      |    |        |
 6249:          |   Side-effect: col removed from       |    |        |
 6250:          |   _col_db; _col_cache and             +----+        |
 6251:          |   _schema_cache cleared.              (idempotent;  |
 6252:          |   Join column cannot be removed.)      chainable)   |
 6253:          |                                                     |
 6254:          +-- add_database( db ) ----------> [DB_ADDED] <------+
 6255:                                                 |    |
 6256:              Side-effect: new columns added;    |    | add_database( db )
 6257:              _col_db extended; SQLite cache     |    | (chainable; each
 6258:              invalidated (if any).              +----+  extends the view)
 6259: 
 6260:     Note: COL_REMOVED and DB_ADDED are not mutually exclusive.
 6261:     Both transitions are legal on any valid object, in any order.
 6262: 
 6263:     Illegal triggers (always croak; object state is not changed):
 6264: 
 6265:       Trigger                              Error
 6266:       -----------------------------------  ---------------------------------
 6267:       new( databases => [] )               error_no_databases
 6268:       remove_column( join_column )         error_remove_join_column
 6269:       add_database( non-reference )        error_invalid_database
 6270:       new() with join_col absent from DB   error_join_col_absent
 6271: 
 6272: =head2 FSM 2: SQLite Cache Lifecycle
 6273: 
 6274: Governs the temporary SQLite cache used by the C<backend='sqlite'> and
 6275: C<backend='auto'> join paths.  The cache does not exist until the first
 6276: query on the SQLite path.
 6277: 
 6278:     [ABSENT] <----- add_database( db )
 6279:        |                  |
 6280:        |  (no temp file)  | Side-effect: old DBI handle disconnected;
 6281:        |                  |   _sqlite_cache deleted.
 6282:        |                  |
 6283:        |                  +<-------------------------------------------+
 6284:        |                                                               |
 6285:        | first query on SQLite path                                    |
 6286:        | Side-effect: File::Temp db created in tmpdir;                 |
 6287:        |   DBI connected; sources ATTACHed or spilled;                 |
 6288:        |   _sqlite_cache = { dbh, tmpfile, n, updated, ... }           |
 6289:        v                                                               |
 6290:     [FRESH] <--+                                                       |
 6291:        |        |                                                      |
 6292:        |        | subsequent queries                                   |
 6293:        |        | (cache reused; refaddr of _sqlite_cache unchanged)   |
 6294:        +--------+                                                      |
 6295:        |                                                               |
 6296:        | updated() timestamp of any source DA changes                  |
 6297:        | -- OR -- source row count changes                             |
 6298:        | Side-effect: none yet (_cache_fresh returns false)            |
 6299:        v                                                               |
 6300:     [STALE]                                                            |
 6301:        |                                                               |
 6302:        | next query                                                    |
 6303:        | Side-effect: old DBI handle disconnected; old temp file       |
 6304:        |   unlinked; new temp file built from current source data.     |
 6305:        +---------------------------------------------------------------+
 6306:        (transitions to FRESH)
 6307: 
 6308:     On object DESTROY:
 6309:       FRESH/STALE:  DBI handle disconnected; File::Temp object released
 6310:                     (temp file unlinked by File::Temp DESTROY).
 6311:       ABSENT:       No temp file exists; no-op.
 6312: 
 6313: =head2 FSM 3: Column Visibility (per column)
 6314: 
 6315: Each column in the logical view independently follows a two-state machine.
 6316: Transition from VISIBLE to REMOVED is one-way: C<add_database> never
 6317: restores a column that is in C<_removed_cols>.
 6318: 
 6319:     [VISIBLE] <-- initial state for every column at construction
 6320:          |    |
 6321:          |    | query / columns() / schema()
 6322:          |    | (column present in results; no state change)
 6323:          +----+
 6324:          |
 6325:          | remove_column( col )
 6326:          | Side-effect: col deleted from _col_db;
 6327:          |   _col_cache and _schema_cache cleared.
 6328:          v
 6329:     [REMOVED] <---+
 6330:          |         |
 6331:          |         | remove_column( col ) again
 6332:          |         | (idempotent; no error; no second side-effect)
 6333:          +---------+
 6334: 
 6335:     One-way invariant:
 6336:       If col is in _removed_cols, then add_database( db_that_has_col )
 6337:       does NOT re-add it.  Formal: col_db' = col_db âŠ• { c | c in
 6338:       ran(db.columns) \ {local_jc} \ removed }.
 6339: 
 6340:     Illegal trigger:
 6341:       remove_column( join_column )  -- error_remove_join_column (croaks;
 6342:                                        state unchanged)
 6343:
